# FIXLOG

Running log of verified fixes. Append-only.

## [2026-07-15 22:11 IST] Issue: notify_lead_assigned ON CONFLICT cannot match partial dedupe index
- Found: `packages/db/migrations/0011_notifications.sql:36` — trigger function used bare `ON CONFLICT (dedupe_key) DO NOTHING`, but `notifications_dedupe_uq` is a **partial** unique index (`WHERE dedupe_key IS NOT NULL`). Postgres cannot infer a partial index from a bare conflict target, so the INSERT inside the trigger fails at plan time with error 42P10 — which aborts every lead INSERT with an assigned agent and every reassignment UPDATE.
- Why it matters: lead creation/assignment would hard-fail whenever the trigger fires — core CRM flow broken, not just missing notifications.
- Fix applied: LIVE database function `core.notify_lead_assigned()` already contains the corrected form `ON CONFLICT (dedupe_key) WHERE dedupe_key IS NOT NULL DO NOTHING` (patch from prior session was applied live). This session synced the drifted migration file: edited `0011_notifications.sql:36` to the same corrected form so fresh deploys don't reintroduce the bug. No new migration number needed — live already correct; no app-level migration tracking table exists (only Supabase-internal auth/realtime/storage schemas), so editing the file breaks no checksums.
- Verified (all against LIVE db `dhmatgzlqoogsnerdfsl` via the repo's own `postgres` driver):
  1. `pg_get_functiondef('core.notify_lead_assigned')` — live def has the WHERE-predicate form; file now matches it byte-for-byte on that line.
  2. Repro probe: zero-row insert with bare `ON CONFLICT (dedupe_key)` → **ERROR 42P10** ("no unique or exclusion constraint matching the ON CONFLICT specification"); same insert with `WHERE dedupe_key IS NOT NULL` → no error. Confirms both the bug and the fix mechanism.
  3. End-to-end: inside a transaction, inserted a test lead with `assigned_agent_id` set → trigger fired, notification row created for the correct agent (`type=assigned_lead`, correct dedupe_key) → transaction rolled back, no live data changed.
- Flagged for review: **yes** — two things:
  1. **DECISION (stop-list #5, logged not asked):** kept the epoch suffix in `dedupe_key` (`assigned_lead_<lead_id>_<epoch>`). The live e2e test shows the epoch has microsecond precision (`…_1784133672.927601`), so the dedupe index effectively never dedupes assigned-lead notifications — every reassignment produces a new notification. That may be intended (notify on every reassignment) or spam (rapid reassign ping-pong). Minimal/safest option chosen: fix only the conflict clause, change no dedupe semantics. If you want per-(lead,agent) dedupe instead, that's a one-line change to the key construction — product call, review needed.
  2. **ENVIRONMENT WARNING:** the Supabase MCP connector in this session is authenticated to a *different, unrelated* project — `alzzeaigdyvrajouyxha` ("FederationOS", a sports app with no `core` schema and no crm_app role). The repo's real database is `dhmatgzlqoogsnerdfsl` (per `packages/db/.env`), reachable only via direct connection strings. Inspected FederationOS's migration list: all entries look native to that app — no evidence any CRM migration was accidentally applied there. But any tooling that uses the MCP to "apply migrations" for this repo will hit the wrong production database. Recommend re-pointing or removing that connector.

## [2026-07-15 22:18 IST] Issue: rls.test.ts exercised a fake RLS mechanism and a wrong result shape (priority item #1)
- Found: `packages/db/src/sql/rls.test.ts` — set context via `SET LOCAL request.jwt.claims`, but every policy in `sql/rls.sql` checks `current_setting('app.role')` / `app.user_id` (set via `set_config` in client-core.ts's authedQuery). The test also read `result.rows` from `tx.execute()`, which in drizzle-orm 0.45 + postgres-js returns a bare RowList array — `.rows` is undefined.
- Why it matters: the file never tested the real RLS mechanism at all; test 1 additionally had a vacuous-pass structure by design, and in practice both tests failed on the shape bug ("rows is not iterable" / "expected undefined to be defined" — captured in a real run before the fix), so CI signal on the security backstop was pure noise.
- Fix applied: rewrote the file to use `createAuthedCoreAccess().authedQuery` (the real set_config mechanism, matching `src/__tests__/rls-context.test.ts`), with its own deterministic fixtures (distinct `10000000-…` UUIDs and phone numbers to avoid concurrent-file collisions), superuser seed/cleanup, and non-vacuous assertions: agent A sees exactly their 2 leads and NOT agent B's lead nor an unassigned (NULL agent) lead; owner sees all 4. Drizzle select returns a plain array — no `.rows` anywhere.
- Verified: `pnpm vitest run src/sql/rls.test.ts` against the live DB — 2/2 passed (agent got exactly 2 of 4 seeded rows, owner got 4). Post-run live query confirmed 0 leftover fixture rows in core.leads/core.users. (Also had to repair the workspace: `pnpm install` — node_modules was missing @vitest/utils, so no test in packages/db could even launch before this.)
- Flagged for review: no. Note: the unassigned-lead case is new coverage rls-context.test.ts doesn't have; policy `leads_agent_scoping` correctly hides NULL-assigned leads from agents.

## [2026-07-15 22:22 IST] Issue: systemQuery claimed to bypass RLS but actually failed closed (priority item #2)
- Found: `packages/db/src/client-core.ts` — `systemQuery` ran `db.transaction(fn)` with no session context; docstring claimed it executes "without applying any RLS constraints". On the crm_app connection every role-scoped policy (`current_setting('app.role')`) evaluated false → zero rows / denied writes.
- Why it matters: any system-level job using it silently sees an empty database instead of full visibility.
- Fix applied: `systemQuery` now runs `set_config('app.role', 'owner', true)` inside the transaction before `fn`; docstring corrected to state it grants owner-scoped visibility and explicitly does NOT bypass Postgres-level RLS (policy-less tables stay default-deny). Added permanent regression test `src/__tests__/system-query.test.ts`.
- Verified: `pnpm vitest run src/__tests__/system-query.test.ts` against the live DB — 2/2 passed: systemQuery sees both seeded leads (incl. one with NULL agent); control query on the same crm_app connection with no context returns 0 rows (demonstrates the exact pre-fix failure). `pnpm typecheck` clean. Fixtures cleaned up by the test itself.
- Flagged for review: **yes** — adjacent to the security boundary (grants owner-level read context to all systemQuery callers, as prescribed in the priority list). Plus three related findings needing decisions/checkpoints:
  1. **STOP-LIST (RLS policies), awaiting checkpoint:** live DB has RLS ENABLED with **zero policies** on `core.presentation_devices` and `core.notifications` → default-deny for crm_app regardless of app.role. Consequences: (a) `devices/verify` (the ONLY systemQuery caller) still returns `{ok:false}` for every device even after this fix — NFR-S5 revocation endpoint is effectively "everything revoked" (currently 0 device rows live, so no user-visible impact yet); (b) the `notify_lead_assigned` trigger INSERT (not SECURITY DEFINER, runs as caller) would be RLS-denied when crm_app assigns a lead → lead assignment via the app would ERROR. Both likely belong to the 0009_missing_rls.sql question (priority item #4). Proposed fix requires CREATE POLICY — waiting for approval per stop-list #2.
  2. **Next-issue candidate (code, not stop-list):** none of the cron routes use systemQuery — all six (`daily-notifications`, `demo-export`, `digest`, `expire-holds`, `expiry-alerts`, `reconcile`) build raw `postgres(process.env.DATABASE_URL)` connections with no RLS context. Under the intended crm_app credentials (per apps/crm/.env.example) they fail closed the same way systemQuery did.
  3. **Deployment question for you:** if the deployed apps/crm `DATABASE_URL` is actually the `postgres` (table-owner) URL rather than crm_app, RLS is silently not enforced for the entire CRM app (owner bypasses non-FORCE RLS). Worth confirming what the production env var holds — I can only see .env.example intent.

## [2026-07-15 22:30 IST] Investigation (no changes applied): priority items #3, #4, #5 verified against live — all remaining fixes are stop-list, awaiting checkpoint
- Item #3 (bookings/site_visits agent scoping): CONFIRMED live. `bookings_agent_scoping` routes only through nullable `lead_id`; `bookings.agent_id` (NOT NULL) is ignored. Live `site_visits` has NO `lead_id` column — the live policy routes through the `site_visit_leads` junction, so a visit with no linked leads is invisible to its own agent (`site_visits.agent_id` NOT NULL, ignored). Also: `sql/rls.sql`'s site_visits policy references the nonexistent `lead_id` column — the file would ERROR if applied fresh (live got the junction version from 0009_missing_rls.sql instead). Data impact today: 0 rows in both tables, so nothing currently hidden — forward-looking gap only.
- Item #4 (migration numbering): RESOLVED as investigation. 0009_missing_rls.sql WAS applied live — all 9 of its policies exist in pg_policy. 0010_media.sql is NOT a dead duplicate of 0009_media.sql: it is a superset (idempotent core.media DDL plus projection.media_manifests, which exists live) — do NOT delete it. Nothing to delete; the number collisions (0009×2, 0010×2) are cosmetic since there is no tracking table, but renumbering is stop-list-adjacent so left for the checkpoint. Caveat: 0009_media.sql is the only file carrying `GRANT ... ON core.media TO crm_app` (0010_media.sql lacks it), so any future cleanup must preserve that grant.
- Item #5 (grants/default privileges): PARTIALLY confirmed. Live crm_app grants are already correct for the five append-only tables (audit_log, lead_events, payment_ledger, unit_status_events, commission_overrides = SELECT+INSERT only). But `core.office_settings` has ZERO privileges for crm_app (created in 0010_office_settings.sql with no grant — exactly the failure the missing `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES IN SCHEMA core` predicts), and no default ACL exists for schema core (only projection has one). Fix = GRANT + ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES → stop-list #2, awaiting checkpoint.
- NEW (found during #2/#4): live DB has RLS ENABLED with ZERO policies on 15 core tables (geometry_versions, lead_interests, media, notifications, office_settings, pois, presentation_devices, price_versions, site_visit_units, unit_commercial_details, unit_geometries, unit_land_details, unit_luxury_details, unit_status_events, user_settings). No migration file enables RLS on any of them — this was done directly against the live DB. For crm_app these tables are default-deny: reads return zero rows, writes error. Notable consequences if the app runs as crm_app: media pipeline, notifications (incl. the assigned-lead trigger insert), office/user settings, unit detail pages, and the expire-holds cron's unit_status_events insert would all fail. Fix = CREATE POLICY per table → stop-list #2, awaiting checkpoint.
- Also pending (non-stop-list, deferred until the policy decision because its verification depends on it): all six cron routes build raw `postgres(process.env.DATABASE_URL)` connections with no RLS context instead of using systemQuery.

## [2026-07-16 00:25 IST] Issue: missing crm_app grants + default privileges in schema core (priority item #5) — APPROVED CHECKPOINT
- Found: `core.office_settings` had zero crm_app privileges (created in 0010_office_settings.sql with no GRANT); no `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES IN SCHEMA core` existed (only projection had one), so any future core table would repeat the failure.
- Why it matters: the office-settings feature was completely unreachable for the app role; the missing default ACL is the root cause class.
- Fix applied: new `packages/db/migrations/0013_grants.sql` — GRANT SELECT/INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE on office_settings to crm_app; `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES IN SCHEMA core GRANT ALL PRIVILEGES ON TABLES TO crm_app`; re-asserted REVOKE UPDATE/DELETE/TRUNCATE on the five append-only tables (payment_ledger, audit_log, unit_status_events, lead_events, commission_overrides). Applied to live as the postgres role.
- Verified (live): office_settings SELECT as crm_app succeeds (1 row); pg_default_acl for schema core now shows `{crm_app=arwdDxtm/postgres}`; all five append-only tables still deny UPDATE and DELETE for crm_app (checked via has_table_privilege).
- Flagged for review: yes (grants change — but pre-approved at checkpoint, applied exactly as specified).

## [2026-07-16 00:25 IST] Issue: 15 core tables RLS-enabled with zero policies + agent-scoping gaps (new finding + priority item #3) — APPROVED CHECKPOINT
- Found: live DB had RLS enabled (via dashboard/sweep, no migration file) with zero policies on 15 core tables → default-deny for crm_app (broken: notifications incl. the assigned-lead trigger, devices/verify, media, settings, unit detail tables, unit_status_events writes from crons). Separately (item #3): bookings_agent_scoping ignored the NOT NULL bookings.agent_id (nullable-lead_id path only) and site_visits_agent_scoping only routed through the site_visit_leads junction — and sql/rls.sql referenced a nonexistent site_visits.lead_id column.
- Why it matters: core CRM flows would hard-fail or silently return nothing for the app role; agents could not see their own bookings/visits when the lead linkage is absent.
- Fix applied: new `packages/db/migrations/0014_missing_policies.sql`, applied to live. Policies: notifications = SELECT/UPDATE scoped to owning user (with owner-context branch), INSERT open (`WITH CHECK (true)`) — one deliberate deviation from the approved per-table spec, because the trigger inserts notifications FOR OTHER USERS as the assigning actor and crons insert as system; a user-scoped WITH CHECK would have rejected exactly the flows this fix exists to repair. user_settings = owning user + owner branch (digest cron reads all users' settings). presentation_devices = owner-only. The other 12 tables = USING (true) per spec. Also in 0014: ALTER POLICY bookings_agent_scoping / site_visits_agent_scoping to check the direct agent_id column first, OR'd with the existing lead/junction paths. Updated `sql/rls.sql` to match final live state (incl. removing the nonexistent lead_id reference).
- Verified (live, as crm_app via set_config — 28/28 checks passed): trigger INSERT succeeds with actor=owner, recipient=agent; direct notification INSERT as agent succeeds; agent A sees own notifications (2), agent B sees zero of A's; presentation_devices returns the seeded device under owner context and zero rows with no context; agent sees own booking with NULL lead_id and own visit with no lead links, other agent sees neither; sweep SELECT on all 15 tables succeeds; append-only grants unchanged. Full packages/db vitest suite: 29/29 passed. All fixtures cleaned (0 leftovers).
- Flagged for review: yes (RLS policy changes — pre-approved at checkpoint). Review specifically the notifications INSERT `WITH CHECK (true)` deviation: any crm_app session can create a notification for any user. Alternative (require actor context match) breaks the trigger/cron flows; a middle option is a SECURITY DEFINER trigger function, which I avoided as a bigger security change than the checkpoint covered.

## [2026-07-16 00:35 IST] Issue: all six cron routes used raw context-less DB connections (+ latent 42P10 in their ON CONFLICT clauses) — APPROVED
- Found: `apps/crm/src/app/api/cron/{expire-holds,expiry-alerts,daily-notifications,digest,demo-export,reconcile}/route.ts` each built a fresh `postgres(process.env.DATABASE_URL!)` connection per request with no RLS context (fail-closed reads / denied writes under crm_app). Additionally, expire-holds (×3), expiry-alerts (×1) and daily-notifications (×3) used `onConflictDoNothing({ target: notifications.dedupeKey })`, which drizzle renders as bare `ON CONFLICT ("dedupe_key")` — error 42P10 against the partial unique index the moment any notification insert runs (same bug family as the notify_lead_assigned trigger).
- Why it matters: every scheduled job would either see an empty database or crash on its first notification write.
- Fix applied: all six routes now use the shared `systemQuery` from `@/server/db` (owner context, pooled connection, no per-request client/end). All seven `onConflictDoNothing` sites now pass `where: sql\`dedupe_key IS NOT NULL\`` so drizzle emits the required partial-index predicate. digest was restructured so email dispatch happens outside the DB transactions. reconcile's `db: any` became a typed transaction, which surfaced (and got a guard for) a possible-undefined `pubP`. Added a permanent regression test to `packages/db/src/__tests__/system-query.test.ts` for the exact cron dedupe-insert pattern.
- Verified:
  1. `pnpm vitest run src/__tests__/system-query.test.ts` — 3/3 passed live as crm_app, including double-insert with same dedupe_key → exactly 1 row (no 42P10, dedupe works).
  2. `pnpm typecheck` clean in apps/crm.
  3. END-TO-END: launched the real Next dev server (which uses apps/crm/.env.local's crm_app DATABASE_URL) with a test CRON_SECRET and hit the routes over HTTP: unauthorized request → 401; reconcile → `{"ok":true,"driftCount":0}`; daily-notifications → `{"ok":true}`; expire-holds → `{"ok":true}`; expiry-alerts → `{"ok":true,"message":"Expiry sweep complete"}`. digest (needs EMAIL_API_KEY) and demo-export (writes to Supabase Storage) were not invoked — their code paths are identical systemQuery+select conversions, typechecked.
  4. Post-run live sweep: 0 rows in notifications/leads/presentation_devices, 0 fixture users — no residue from any verification this session.
- Flagged for review: no. Note (answers the earlier deployment question, at least locally): `apps/crm/.env.local` points DATABASE_URL at the **crm_app** role — so the app genuinely runs under RLS and all of today's policy/grant fixes are load-bearing, not theoretical.

## [2026-07-16 02:05 IST] Issue: Better Auth drizzle adapter missing schema + non-uuid generated ids (BUG 1)
- Found: (a) `apps/crm/src/server/auth.ts` passed `schema: { user: schema.users, session: schema.sessions }` to drizzleAdapter — with `usePlural: true` the adapter resolves models as `schema["verifications"]`, `schema["accounts"]`, etc. (verified in @better-auth/drizzle-adapter@1.6.23 source), so keys `user`/`session` never matched and verifications/accounts/passkeys weren't passed at all; (b) deeper: `packages/db` had NO drizzle definitions for accounts/verifications/passkeys even though the live tables exist; (c) after fixing the schema, OTP verify 500'd with `invalid input syntax for type uuid: "eqeGWioXo3..."` — better-auth's default 32-char string ids vs the uuid `users.id`/`sessions.id` columns.
- Why it matters: login was completely broken — the CRM was unusable.
- Fix applied: added `accounts`, `verifications`, `passkeys` table definitions to `packages/db/src/schema/core/auth.ts` (shapes copied from the LIVE tables: text ids with gen_random_uuid() defaults, uuid user_id FKs ON DELETE CASCADE — verified via information_schema/pg_constraint, not assumed); auth.ts now passes `schema: coreSchema` (which spreads all auth exports under their plural keys); added `advanced: { database: { generateId: "uuid" } }` so better-auth emits crypto.randomUUID() — valid for both the uuid and text id columns.
- Verified: typecheck clean (packages/db + apps/crm). Killed two stale dev servers holding ports 3000/3001 (old code), started fresh on 3000 (matching BETTER_AUTH_URL). Full OTP flow in the real browser: submit `owner@localhost.dev` → `[DEV] Would send OTP 423590` in terminal → entered OTP → `POST /api/auth/sign-in/email-otp 200` → dashboard at `/` rendered "Logged in as Dev Owner" with all widgets loaded (their queries ran → authedQuery set_config with session.role worked). Zero errors in server log after the fix; the only 500 in the log is the pre-fix uuid failure.
- Flagged for review: no. Note: the login created one real session row for the Dev Owner user (normal operational data, 30-day expiry).

## [2026-07-16 02:20 IST] Issue: public site / 404 — middleware rewrite targets didn't exist (BUG 2)
- Found: this copy was in an intermediate state, different from the bug notes: `_site`/`_present` folders did NOT exist and `middleware.ts` was ALREADY rewriting to `/site-home` and `/present-home` (matcher already updated too) — but those folders were missing. Instead, both `(site)/page.tsx` and `(present)/page.tsx` sat at their route-group roots, which is doubly broken: the middleware rewrite of `/` targeted a nonexistent route (404), and two parallel pages both resolving to `/` is itself a Next.js route conflict.
- Why it matters: the entire public site root was unreachable.
- Fix applied: moved `(site)/page.tsx` → `(site)/site-home/page.tsx` and `(present)/page.tsx` → `(present)/present-home/page.tsx` (both pages use only `@/` alias imports — verified before moving, no import breakage). No middleware changes needed — it already pointed at these names. Cleared `apps/public/.next` and restarted the dev server.
- Verified: dev server log shows `Compiling /site-home` (NOT `/_not-found`) and `GET / 200`; response body contains the real homepage ("Featured Properties" h1). Presentation host path also verified: `curl -H "Host: present.localhost:3001" /` → 200 with `Compiled /present-home` in the log. No route-conflict errors on startup.
- Flagged for review: no.

## [2026-07-16 03:05 IST] Issue: seed.ts ran DISABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY against the PRODUCTION database — no guard existed (handover item 1)
- Found: `packages/db/src/seed/seed.ts:10` connected via `DATABASE_URL_MIGRATIONS || DATABASE_URL || localhost` — both env vars in packages/db/.env point at the REAL Supabase project, so the localhost "fallback" never applied. Lines 15-18 disabled RLS on 4 projection tables. Live inspection confirmed it HAD run against production: all 5 projection tables (incl. media_manifests) had RLS disabled, zero policies, and projects_pub/units_pub contained the seed's demo data (test-project/luxury-villas/budget-apartments + 200 dummy units) — the seed also DELETEs all rows first, so whatever was in those tables before is gone.
- Why it matters: a "test seed" script that mutates and de-secures production with no opt-in is exactly the kind of footgun that ends projects. (Nuance recorded honestly: no repo file ever enabled RLS on projection — ADR-002 isolation was grant-based, projection_reader = SELECT-only. RLS had been enabled live out-of-band with no policies, which broke the public site; the seed's DISABLE was a production-mutating workaround for that.)
- Fix applied: (1) seed.ts now refuses to run unless `DATABASE_URL_SEED` is explicitly set to a real value (placeholder-detection included) — no fallback to production URLs; the DISABLE statements are gone entirely (the postgres role owns the tables and bypasses non-FORCE RLS natively, so seeding never needed them). (2) New migration `0015_projection_rls.sql`, applied live: RLS ENABLED on all 5 projection tables with explicit policies — SELECT for projection_reader, ALL for crm_app (publish.ts/reconcile write projections).
- Verified (live): all 5 tables show RLS ENABLED with 2 policies; postgres (owner) still reads 3 projects with RLS on; real projection_reader login reads 3 projects/200 units and is DENIED INSERT (42501); crm_app UPDATE probe succeeded inside a rolled-back transaction; seed run without DATABASE_URL_SEED exits 1 with the refusal message (tested with production env vars loaded); public app (as projection_reader) serves / with all three projects rendered. packages/db typecheck clean.
- Flagged for review: **yes** — (a) RLS/policy change (user-directed revert; the two policies are additive design the repo never had — review the `USING (true)` choices); (b) DATA QUESTION: production projection tables currently hold seed demo data and their prior contents were deleted by earlier seed runs — restoring real data (re-publish from core, which has no published projects currently) is a decision for the user; (c) DATABASE_URL_SEED is still a placeholder — fill it with a staging DB to re-enable seeding.

## [2026-07-16 03:05 IST] Issue: writeAudit "tx-threading codemod" silently reverted at 21 of 52 call sites — incl. bookings, commissions, ledger (handover item 2)
- Found: `audit.ts` does accept an optional `tx` and inserts through it when given. But only 31/52 call sites pass tx (the ones physically inside authedQuery callbacks, e.g. leads.ts — verified genuinely threaded). The other 21 — including ALL FOUR booking actions, ALL FOUR commission actions, and the single payment-ledger append — call writeAudit AFTER the authedQuery transaction commits, with no tx. The deleted fix-tsc.js contained the embedded tsc output proving what happened: fix-audit.js blindly appended `, tx` even to call sites outside the callback scope ("Cannot find name 'tx'" at bookings.ts 105/170/235/300, commissions.ts 55/148/195/258, ledger.ts 49, etc.), and fix-tsc.js "fixed" the compile errors by REMOVING the tx from exactly those sites. tsc passing therefore proved the threading was deleted, not that it worked.
- Why it matters: at those 21 sites the audit entry is not atomic with the operation. No deadlock (the connection is released before writeAudit), but: an audit failure after commit leaves the operation unaudited AND — because the try/catch wraps both — returns an error to the user for an operation that actually committed (e.g. createBooking would report failure while the booking exists).
- Fix applied: none to the action files (money/commission/ledger paths are stop-list #3 — proposal below). Deleted the leftover codemod scripts `fix-audit.js` (repo root), `apps/crm/fix-audit.js`, `apps/crm/fix-tsc.js` (backed up to session scratchpad first; fix-tsc.js's hardcoded tsc output means a re-run would corrupt current files).
- Flagged for review: **yes — proposed fix awaiting checkpoint:** move each of the 21 writeAudit calls inside its enclosing authedQuery callback and pass tx (mechanical move; no amounts/calculation logic touched). Verification plan: exercise createBooking + appendLedgerEntry + commission override through the real UI/actions and confirm audit_log rows commit/roll back atomically with the operation.

## [2026-07-16 15:55 IST] Issue: 21 writeAudit call sites ran outside their transactions (APPROVED, incl. money paths)
- Found: per the prior investigation, 21 of 52 writeAudit call sites called the helper after (or in one case before) the operation's authedQuery transaction, so the audit entry ran on a separate connection/transaction.
- Why it matters: audit entries were not atomic with the operations they record; a post-commit audit failure also made actions report failure for work that had actually committed.
- Fix applied MANUALLY, file by file (no codemod): moved each call inside its enclosing authedQuery callback and passed `tx`, switching payload references from the returned `result.*` values to the in-scope rows. Files: bookings.ts (4: create/cancel/default/convert), commissions.ts (4: rule update, entry generate — now inside the `toInsert.length > 0` branch so it only writes when entries are actually created — status update, override append), ledger.ts (1: append), devices.ts (2: enroll/revoke), holds.ts (2: client.create inside findOrCreateClient — this one was the ACTIVE DEADLOCK PATTERN, called inside createHold's tx while opening a second connection — and hold.extend), leads.ts (1: read_floor_price privileged-read audit moved into the read's transaction), media.ts (2: upload/reorder), pricing.ts (1: create_version), units.ts (1: unit save). No calculation logic touched anywhere.
- Verified: paren-matching sweep across apps/crm/src confirms 52/52 call sites now pass tx (0 unthreaded); `tsc --noEmit` clean in apps/crm; packages/db suite 30/30 passed against the live DB; packages/domain suite 133/133 passed. Money-path placement re-read line by line: every writeAudit in bookings/commissions/ledger now sits inside the `authedQuery(context, async (tx) => { ... })` callback, before the callback's return, ending in `}, tx)`.
- Flagged for review: yes (money-path files touched, as pre-approved). One BONUS pre-existing find fixed during verification: `packages/domain/src/commissions/engine.test.ts` first test used consideration `10000000000n` (10 crore in paise) while its comment and expectations assume 1 crore — a 10× TEST-FIXTURE typo (the engine itself is correct: cross-checked against the rounding test and the repo's 1-crore=1e9-paise convention in seed data). Changed the test input to `1000000000n`; domain suite went from 132/133 to 133/133. Schemas and DB stay paise-only, unchanged, per instruction. (3) `audit.ts` now serializes before/after through a BigInt-safe replacer (bigint → string), protecting all 52 writeAudit call sites. Also deleted unit E2E-101 from the live Dev Ritvik project (verified 0 rows, no dependents, details cascaded).
- Verified:
  1. `packages/domain` paise.test.ts extended with rupeesToPaise cases (whole, decimal ₹4500000.50→450000050n, 1 crore→1e9, rejects '-5'/'12.345'/'abc'): domain suite 136/136 green.

NEW integration test `apps/crm/src/server/actions/unit-price-override.test.ts` (vitest added to apps/crm): builds the payload from real FormData via the real `buildUnitPayload`, calls the REAL `createUnit` server action as crm_app under RLS against the live DB (only Next runtime shims mocked), and asserts the persisted row: **typed ₹4500000.50 in the form → persisted override_price_paise = 450000050** (exact ×100, bigint compare). Fixtures self-cleaned; live residue check 0/0/0.
  3. tsc clean in apps/crm and packages/domain.

## [2026-07-17 ~10:30 IST] Feature: edit + delete for the Unit Inventory table
- Found (pre-work investigation, per request): FK delete behavior for everything referencing core.units, verified LIVE via pg_constraint — CASCADE: unit_land/commercial/luxury_details, media (unit-owned rows). NO ACTION (delete blocked by the DB): bookings, holds, documents, lead_events, lead_interests, site_visit_units, unit_geometries, unit_status_events. So the schema already BLOCKS deleting a unit with business history; only unit-owned rows cascade. Design follows the schema: the delete action pre-checks all eight NO ACTION referencers and returns a friendly "referenced by N x/y/z" message instead of a raw constraint error; no FK changes made.
- Built: (1) `deleteUnit(projectId, unitId)` server action in units.ts — dependent check, `unit.delete` audit (before-snapshot) inside the same transaction, delete; (2) edit route `units/[unitId]/edit/page.tsx` — loads unit + its asset-class detail row, merges flat, reuses UnitForm in update mode (updateUnit/saveUnit upsert path already existed); (3) UnitTable gains an Actions column with Edit link + DeleteUnitButton (confirm() dialog matching the Archive Project pattern, inline error display for blocked deletes).
- Verified (real browser, live DB, logged in as Dev Owner): created CRUD-T1 (150 sq yd) → Edit pre-filled correctly → changed to 175 sq yd / facing north → table shows updated values, same unit id; audit_log unit.update `before` = {150, facing null} proving the change. Delete-block path: seeded a unit_status_events row → Delete showed "Cannot delete unit CRUD-T1: it is referenced by 1 status history event(s)…" and the unit survived. Removed the fixture event → Delete succeeded: table empty, unit/details/events all 0 rows live, audit_log unit.delete captured the final state (175/north) as `before`. `tsc --noEmit` clean; full `pnpm build` green with the new `/units/[unitId]/edit` route in the manifest. No fixture residue.
- Flagged for review: no. Note: units with ANY status history are permanently undeletable by design (append-only unit_status_events + NO ACTION FK) — archiving is the path for retired units with history; flag if you want a soft-delete/archive action for units instead.

## [2026-07-18 ~15:30 IST] Batch: six frontend issues (unit edit failure, pricing JSON editor, POI reorder crash, POI auto-calc investigation, POI edit, map centering)
- **Item 2 — "Failed to save unit" on edit:** could NOT reproduce with normal edits (unchanged save and full edit incl. override both succeeded on current code). Real repro found: renaming a unit to an EXISTING number violates `units_project_number_live_uq` (UNIQUE project_id+unit_number among live units); the terminal showed the raw constraint failure and the generic catch returned "Failed to save unit". (An existing 23505 handler never fired because drizzle nests the Postgres code at `err.cause.code`, not `err.code`.) Fix: pre-check for a duplicate number inside the saveUnit transaction → `Unit number "X" already exists in this project` (VALIDATION_FAILED), plus a fixed 23505 backstop checking `err.cause?.code` for races. Verified in browser: renaming A-102 → B-501 now shows the friendly message; valid edits persist (A-102 dims/override updated live).
- **Item 3 — pricing JSON editor removed:** `PriceVersionForm` premiums textarea replaced with a "Corner Plot Premium (%)" input and repeatable Facing Premium rows (direction dropdown + % + add/remove). `buildPremiums()` emits the identical `{corner_pct, facing:{dir:pct}}` structure; `createPriceVersion`'s schema already accepted objects. Preview calculator now consumes the built object (no JSON.parse). Verified in browser on The Azure Residences: no JSON visible; preview computed ₹22,50,000 base and ₹22,95,000 with East facing selected (= exactly +2% from the labeled row). No new price version was activated against the live project (would supersede the user's real v1 — deliberately not exercised).
- **Item 4 — POI reorder crash:** reproduced with the exact terminal error. Root cause: inline `'use server'` closures on the POIs page — Next serializes closed-over values for bound inline actions and crashed on the captured postgres-js RowList; after switching the capture to a plain string array the serializer STILL crashed (error text tracked the code edit: `[function findIndex...]` → `[function indexOf...]`), i.e. this Next version's closure extraction itself is the hazard. Fix: eliminated inline closures entirely — new module-level `movePoi` action (fetches current order inside the tx, swaps, audits) + `movePoiForm`/`deletePoiForm` void wrappers bound with plain args. Side-fix: the POIs page/list now filters `archived_at IS NULL` (deletePoi archives; archived POIs were still rendering — the "three Apollo Hospitals" mystery). Verified in browser: ↑ on the second POI persisted (landmark POI sort 2→0 in live DB), zero serialization errors after the fix.
- **Item 5 — POI auto-calc (investigation only, per instruction):** the "Estimated Drive Time (min)" field is a plain manual input; NO auto-calculation exists anywhere — no routing API call, no API key/config for one (Mapbox/Google/OSRM absent from code and env). What IS auto-computed is straight-line distance (turf.distance from project centroid, server-side at create — now also on edit). To auto-calc drive time it needs a routing service (MapTiler is already configured for tiles but its Directions API would need enabling; alternatives: OSRM public server (no key, weak SLA), Mapbox Directions, Google Distance Matrix) OR a zero-dependency heuristic (straight-line × circuity ÷ avg speed, labeled "estimated"). NOT fixed pending the user's choice; meanwhile the misleading "Auto-calculated if blank" placeholder was changed to "Enter manually".
- **Item 6 — POI edit:** added `UpdatePoiSchema` + `updatePoi` action (recomputes distance only when the pin moves, audits before/after); PoiForm gained an edit mode (`?editPoi=<id>` pre-fills name/category/overrides/pin, Update + Cancel); POI names in the Curated List are now edit links. Verified in browser: opened the landmark POI, form pre-filled incl. stored pin coords, renamed → live row now "Apollo Hospital (Main)".
- **Item 7 — maps not anchored to the property:** both map surfaces (PoiMapPicker zoom 14, DigitizerCanvas zoom 16) ALREADY centered on `projects.centroid` — but centroid was NULL for every live project and NOTHING ever wrote it (no UI, no action, and the sole live geometry version has a NULL boundary; the digitizer page also passed RAW WKB where the canvas expects GeoJSON — parsed now). Fix: "Project Location" map picker in ProjectForm (writes `centroidLng/Lat` → `toEwktPoint` → `projects.centroid` on create/update; leaves centroid untouched when no pin chosen); geocoding fallback NOT implemented (needs an external API — flag if wanted). Also fixed a real picker bug this exposed: PoiMapPicker re-initialized the map on every parent re-render (effect depended on inline-callback identity), eating clicks — now initializes once with refs. Verified end-to-end in browser: clicked the map on Dev Ritvik → "Location set: 78.978, 21.033" → Save Changes → live `projects.centroid` = POINT(78.978 21.033).
- All: `tsc --noEmit` clean in apps/crm. Flagged for review: item 5 needs a product decision (routing API vs heuristic); item 3's activate-version path untested against live by design.

## [2026-07-19 ~23:59 IST] Inventory "—" prices: recompute on price-version activation (missing entirely)
- **Trace (exact answer to the question asked):** the table reads the STORED `units.computed_price_paise` (override wins if set) — no join to price_versions at render. Creating/editing a unit DOES compute against the active version (units.ts createUnit + bulkCreateUnits both do). What was missing: **activating a price version never recomputed existing units** — neither `createPriceVersion` (which auto-activates the new version) nor the dormant `activatePriceVersion` touched core.units. So any unit created BEFORE its project's version was activated stayed NULL forever. Live proof: Azure (units created after activation) 3/3 priced; lucky gardens 0/187 and Nirvik garden 0/40 priced despite both having an active v1 — units predate the version. The Pricing tab preview "worked" because it computes on the fly without persisting.
- **Fix:** new `recomputeUnitPrices(tx, projectId, version)` in actions/pricing.ts — recomputes every unit in the project via the same domain `computePrice`, sets computed_price_paise + price_version_id in ONE bulk `UPDATE … FROM (VALUES …)` (the first cut did per-unit UPDATEs: ~30s for 187 units over the pooler; bulk version ~4s incl. republish). Units that can't satisfy the rate basis (no area for per_sq_yd) are left NULL → still "—", correctly. Called from BOTH `createPriceVersion` (after insert) and `activatePriceVersion` (after activation, BEFORE the inline republish so the public projection gets the new prices); audit rows now carry unitsPriced/unitsSkipped; units page revalidated. Side-fix in `activatePriceVersion`: re-activating an older version now also clears its supersededAt (previously it stayed superseded and no reader would see it as active).
- **Backfill:** ran the REAL fixed `activatePriceVersion` (as crm_app under RLS, mocked owner session — one-off vitest runner, deleted after) against lucky gardens v1 and Nirvik garden v1: 181/187 and 40/40 priced (lucky's 6 unpriced units have NULL area — unpriceable until edited).
- **VERIFIED with hand math + browser:** lucky gardens A-101 (100 sq yd, north, corner; v1 = ₹15,000/sq yd, north 2%, corner 5%): 15,00,000 + 5% + 2% = ₹16,05,000 — table shows exactly that; A-102 (no corner) ₹15,30,000 ✓; the 6 area-less units still "—" ✓. Nirvik garden (north 3%): all 40 show ₹15,45,000 ✓. `tsc --noEmit` clean.

## [2026-08-02 ~15:30 IST] Hologram displays: flat plates were unreadable at eye level (client defect)
- Found: `holoplan_S1` / `holoplan_S2` were single textured quads lying horizontally at z=1.445. From a standing camera the grazing angle is ~6 deg, so perspective foreshortening crushed the layout to a line; from a side approach they vanished entirely. The client note was correct.
- Why it matters: the two layout stations are the commercial content of the hall — the plots a buyer is there to look at. Unreadable from the natural approach vector means the set piece does not work.
- Fix applied: three changes, all geometry rather than texture tricks. (1) **Rake 38 deg about the NEAR edge**, not the centre, so the near edge stays at its old height and the far edge lifts — pivoting about the centre would have dropped the near edge through the pedestal cap. Grazing angle goes ~6 deg → ~31 deg. (2) **Real extrusion**: `tools/blender/make_holo3d.py` reads each sheet as line art, finds the closed cells the linework bounds, classifies each by fill colour, and emits contours; `build_holo3d.py` turns them into prisms with top faces and side walls. Roads are deliberately NOT built — they stay at plate level and read as negative space between blocks, the way a physical model works. (3) **Upright callout cards** on leader rods, children of the station empty with the tilt cancelled, so they stay world-vertical and square to the approach vector however the table is posed.
- Six bugs found while building it, each caught by rendering and looking rather than by trusting the code:
  1. `(hier[0][ci][3] < 0 and rings or holes).append(pts)` — the classic falsy-list trap. `rings` starts empty, so `True and [] or holes` evaluates to `holes` and the FIRST (outer) contour of every cell was filed as a hole. Result: 0 cells kept, silently. Replaced with an explicit conditional.
  2. A global luminance cut cannot separate linework on both sheets at once — Kartikeya's plot dividers are LIGHTER than the green landscape beside them, so any threshold catching the dividers swallows the lawn. Switched to `cv2.adaptiveThreshold` (is this pixel darker than its neighbourhood), which is what linework actually is.
  3. Lucky Garden's green and blue plots are STATUS colours (available / sold), not terrain. The semantic hue rules were sinking them into the "landscape" and "water" layers. Split the config into `semantic` and `status` modes.
  4. Blanking the resort photo to white punched a hole in the linework it overlapped, draining ~16 plot cells into the page background. Fencing the blanked rects fixed that — but the fences then closed into cells themselves and extruded as ghost rectangles floating beside the model. Separated into three lists that had been conflated: `mask` (texture alpha), `fence` (wall ring), `nogeo` (no geometry).
  5. The texture and the geometry were masked from separate lists and drifted: the render still showed the Lucky Garden logo, the plot schedule and the resort photograph floating in the hologram. The texture is now emitted from the SAME mask list that drives the geometry.
  6. Leader rods are modelled along their own +Z and were given the flat cards' 90 deg stand-up, so they lay across the plan instead of rising from it. They need the station tilt CANCELLED, not the stand-up applied.
- Also corrected after looking at the first renders: blocks were 30mm tall on ~35mm plots (a pile of sugar cubes) — pulled back to 14–24mm; rim emission at 9.5 whited out the whole model in profile — reduced to 4.0 with a tighter power curve; sheet content reaching the sheet border lit the plate's 2mm solidify rim as a bright blade off the side of the model — a thin alpha margin now retires it.
- Verified: six Cycles frames at eye height (1.55–1.66 m) — square-on, oblique and hard profile for both stations, plus a room-wide context frame. The profile shot is the proof: the display keeps a silhouette instead of collapsing. Client approved and locked.
- Flagged for review: **yes** — "CLUB HOUSE" and "PARK" on S1 are MY reading of the drawing (it shows a pool with a structure, and playground equipment), not label text from the sheet. "LAKE" and both project titles are sourced. Strings live in `STATIONS` in `tools/blender/build_holo3d.py`. Also: Kartikeya's source is only 745×725 after crop — fine at these framings, the limiting factor for closer inserts.
