  1. **The hedge grew 620 → 750 vertices on an UNCHANGED 3,234 indices** — UV seam splitting, not a different object. Rather than reuse `--allow-growth` (which exists for a deliberate change of extent), the tool now recognises this case on its own evidence: bounds within 5 mm **and** identical index count **and** vertex count not decreasing. It is reported as `SEAM-SPLIT` on every run.
  2. **Re-exporting the 14 cypress cones through Blender 5.2 changed their triangulation, 304 → 320 triangles each (+224 total)**, because the exporter fans the cone's n-gon base differently from the build that produced v5. Bounds identical to 5 mm, shape unchanged. `--allow-growth` was passed deliberately for this and the cost is stated.
- **Cost at HERO:** 949 → 951 draw calls, 364,626 → 365,846 submitted triangles, 16 → 17 materials, GPU texture residency **54.65 → 62.65 MB**. The +8 MB is two UASTC normal maps at ~1 MB each on the wire — **P5J's first optimisation target**, on the measured basis already in this log that ETC1S only facets across large flat surfaces and foliage has none. 0 console errors; every Phase 4 control re-verified byte-identical in both patches.

## [2026-09-04 13:35 IST] Phase 5 / P5F–P5G: the arrival needs nothing, and the fountain water was opaque rather than dark

- **P5F — ARRIVAL: a deliberate no-op, recorded rather than skipped.** The arrival itself was delivered by p5a (gravel turning circle + approach) and p5c (its edging). What remained on the list — gate piers where the drive passes between the hedge runs' ends at z 19, a treatment where the approach meets the far field at z 34 — was tested against the mandate's own visibility rule and **rejected**: the HERO camera stands at z 27 looking toward the origin, so both sit behind or below its frame, and the drive measures 0.92% at WEST and 0.41% at NW. Geometry at z 19–34 is not visible at any validated camera. "We built nothing" is a result and it needs its reasoning on the record.
- **P5G — the water was not dark, it was OPAQUE.** In glTF a transmissive material's `baseColorFactor` is its TRANSMISSION TINT — it multiplies what is seen through the surface. `MAT_Water` carried **(0.03, 0.06, 0.07)**, so it passed 3–7% of the light from the basin floor beneath it, and that floor (`fount_floor`, `MAT_Stone_Paving`) renders at L 116 in the open. Masked, the water measured **L 16.41 with sd 3.54** at HERO: a uniform black hole at 4.1% of the frame.
- **The replacement is derived from the geometry, not chosen.** The water surface sits at y 0.40 over a floor at 0.05–0.14, so the depth is **0.26 m** and the light path is twice that. Clear-water absorption (0.270 / 0.050 / 0.020 per metre at 600 / 500 / 450 nm) gives a transmittance of **(0.869, 0.974, 0.990)** — water over a quarter-metre is very nearly colourless with a faint cyan cast. ×0.86 for the suspended matter a stone basin carries, +2% green: **(0.747, 0.858, 0.851)**. The shipped value was **25× / 14× / 12× too dark**.
- **Roughness is untouched, and that is a measurement too.** P4D set it to 0.07 and it is not the limiting factor: the HERO camera looks down on the basin at ~30° of incidence, where an ior 1.333 surface reflects about **2.5%**. Ninety-seven per cent of what the water shows is transmission, so the tint is the whole story and the P4D decision stands.
- **Verified (HERO daylight):** water **L 16.41 → 84.05 (+67.64)**, sd **3.54 → 13.23** — it now shows the basin floor's structure instead of being uniformly dark. WEST **30.85 → 100.68**. Dusk 102.91, sd 20.00. **Every other class moved ≤ 0.04** at HERO: terrain +0.02, hedge +0.04, drive +0.03, fountain stone +0.01, terrace +0.03, masonry 0.00, roof 0.00, mansion +0.01, steps −0.01, cypress +0.01. A single factor change, perfectly isolated; both Phase 4 controls re-verified byte-identical.
- Cost: **zero** — one `baseColorFactor`. No geometry, no texture, no draw call, no byte of payload.

## [2026-09-04 14:30 IST] Phase 5 / P5I-P5J close: an optimisation rejected by its own measurement, and an instrument that was never sound

- **P5J's only optimisation was REJECTED.** The two foliage normal maps are Phase 5's largest single cost, and ETC1S instead of UASTC took them to 336,783 and 297,246 bytes — **−67% and −71%**, 16.60 → 15.27 MB. The repository's own v5etc1s finding said this should be safe: ETC1S facets across large FLAT surfaces and foliage has none. **It was not safe.** Masked against p5g at the same poses: hedge **48.10 → 32.24 (−15.9)** at WEST, cypress **56.74 → 43.54 (−13.2)**; at NW cypress **66.44 → 40.91 (−25.5)** and hedge **61.82 → 45.77 (−16.1)**, with sd rising at the same time (cypress 10.95 → 22.85). Luminance down and deviation up together is the signature: the codec quantises the normal erratically enough to tilt whole clumps off the key, so the planting darkens *and* goes blotchy. 1.3 MB is not worth 25 luma on the largest soft mass in the NW frame. Reverted; a pruned p5j came out byte-identical to p5g and was deleted rather than shipped as a duplicate. **p5g is the Phase 5 candidate.**
- **The per-class luminance instrument was never sound, and the guard is what proved it.** It read the WebGL canvas with `drawImage` inside a rAF. Under `preserveDrawingBuffer:false` that is only valid between the app's draw and the next clear, and whether a callback lands inside that window depends on rAF registration order against r3f's own loop. On the first p5j roll-up, **three of four captures returned every class at L 0, sd 0, rgb (0,0,0)** — and they were one step from being written into this log as measurements. Adding a non-black check turned a silent wrong answer into a loud failure, which is how the cause was found: earlier runs had simply been lucky. Two intermediate fixes made it worse and are recorded because they were informative — waiting a second rAF turned an intermittent failure into a total one (confirming the one-hop window), and publishing the id buffer as a DOM canvas for offline analysis disturbed the page enough to blacken the visible capture too. The probe now reports `shadeUnavailable` rather than zeros; **every number in the Phase 5 report comes from a capture where the guard passed**, and two of the eight final captures hit the unavailable path and are reported as such.
- **P5I — composition:** evaluation only, no candidate. Architectural dominance holds numerically: lawn/mansion value ratio **0.70** before and after (P5A v1 would have compressed it to 0.81 and was corrected). Hierarchy at HERO reads mansion 100.4 > terrace 116.1 / masonry 132.9 above it, drive 94.9 > lawn 70.5 > hedge 53.6 > cypress 44.9 below. NW spends nothing on the invisible — fountain and steps are 0.00% there and no Phase 5 work was aimed at them.
- **P5H — NOT AUTHORED, and stated as an omission rather than a decision.** The one candidate that passes the visibility test is a pair of entrance urns (~33 px at HERO). Budget was exhausted; nothing was started. Recommended as a short follow-up alongside dusk path lighting.
- **Final regression:** typecheck 5/5, **266 tests**, lint 0 errors, clean production build on a deleted `.next`, **0 console errors** on all four exterior captures and on `/hall`. Immutability audit: v5 `d7a7e945…`, p4e `88d46e24…`, p25b*/p31–p34/p4a–p4d/interior all unchanged, locked blend mtime unchanged, `EXTERIOR_MODEL_URL` still v5, and all five Phase 4 stone sources re-verified **byte-identical** against the shipped p4e.
- Full report: `docs/PHASE5_REPORT.md`. **PASS for P5A–P5G and P5I–P5J; P5H not authored. Recommended candidate p5g.**

Bedded 8 mm into the cheek so nothing is coplanar; the script asserts the die lies inside the cheek's **flat top** (it is a chamfered box, so "inside the bbox" is not enough), that nothing floats, and that the pair is symmetric about x=0.

Baked with `bake_ao_raycast.py` at the same 0.40 floor as the rest of the stone (verified first that the module has no import-time side effects — the P4A lesson). Mean **0.714**, which sits exactly where it belongs: finial_plinth 0.812 > finial_urn 0.786 > cymatium 0.753 > terrace_lower 0.726 > **urn 0.714** > terrace_upper 0.702 > cornice 0.630 > the cheek it stands on 0.595. The frame shows why — **two circular pools on the paving with no fixture to explain them**, the exact "sticker" read this rig's own comments already document. And the cost lands on the SHIPPING look: daylight ships, `?grade=dusk` is the rollback, and three's `WebGLLights.setup()` increments `pointLength` **unconditionally** (no `intensity === 0` skip, verified in the installed source), so a dusk-only light still costs a per-fragment loop in daylight. **The urns already deliver the dusk arrival at zero lighting cost** — L 132.03 dusk against 100.85 daylight, because they stand 2.75 m from the entrance rectAreaLight.

The real cause is **winding**: 229 of its 249 polygons carry a normal pointing at the ground (mean normal z **−0.839**) and `MAT_Stone_Trim` is `doubleSided:false`, so nine tenths of it is backface-culled. `p5c_transitions.py` builds each ribbon quad from a point order that depends on traverse direction and **never calls `bmesh.ops.recalc_face_normals`**; its own assertion checks vertex Z against the sampled ground to 1e-4 — a POSITION check, which is true and says nothing about winding. **An assertion that was correct in the authoring space and meaningless in the shipped one.** Cost to the shipped candidate: 498 triangles that draw nothing plus a **fork of `MAT_Stone_Trim`** (it is the only one of 37 trim primitives with no COLOR_0) — so the "+2 materials" Phase 5 was credited with is really one real material and one accident. **Not fixed here**: it is a P5C defect whose fix makes a kerb line *appear* that has never been seen, needing its own four-camera acceptance pass. Fix specified in the report.
- **Two instruments repaired, both validated rather than asserted.** (1) `SETTLE` converged on per-frame movement, which is **frame-rate dependent** — the rig is a first-order lag, so a faster run takes smaller steps and trips a fixed threshold *earlier*, further from the beat. The first p5h run did exactly that at 94–101 rAF/s: pose **0.157/0.174/0.164 m** against p5g's 0.000/0.014/0.024, a **six-pixel shift** between the frames being differenced, larger than the 0.07 % object under test. Those numbers were **discarded and the run repeated**; SETTLE now converges on the pose error itself and all 8 captures report `onBeat`. (2) The visible-frame read moved from `drawImage` through a 2D canvas to `readPixels` on the back buffer, removing the dependency on rAF ordering. Both paths are taken every capture and compared: **8 of 8 succeeded on both, `visAgreeMAD` 0.000**, against 6 of 8 before — so Phase 5 residual #5 is closed by measurement, not by argument.
- **Final regression:** typecheck **5/5**, **266 tests**, lint **5/5 / 0 errors**, clean production build, **0 console errors** on all four exterior captures and `/hall`. Immutability: every pre-existing GLB **byte-identical** (v5 `d7a7e945…`, p4e `88d46e24…`, p5g `4addfaa8…`), all **42** material source PNGs byte-identical, locked blend mtime and size unchanged, **5/5** Phase 4 stone sources MATCH against shipped p4e, `EXTERIOR_MODEL_URL` still v5.

## [2026-09-04 20:15 IST] Phase 5 / completion: P5C's edging repaired at the generator, the cypress normal halved, and the exterior promoted

- **The P5C winding bug was re-derived from the generator, not taken from the report.** `strip()` built each quad as `[p0, p1, q1, q0]` — along the polyline, then across the ribbon — so its sense depends on the sign relationship between travel direction and offset direction. Working the cross product per caller predicts **−Z for rect_run's four sides (132 quads), −Z for the forecourt arc (77) and −Z for the `side=-1` approach flank (20), but +Z for the `side=+1` flank (20)** — because its offset is mirrored. 132+77+40 = **249 polygons, 229 down, 20 up**, which is exactly what the shipped mesh measures. Every polygon accounted for.
- **Fixed at the generator, and deliberately NOT with `recalc_face_normals`.** These ribbons are an OPEN surface: recalc only makes a connected component mutually *consistent*, and for an open sheet the global sense it lands on is arbitrary — that would trade a reproducible bug for a coin toss. Every quad is a near-horizontal strip whose visible face is its top, so the orientation is knowable outright from the quad's own XY shoelace area. Result **asserted**, because the original assertion checked vertex Z against the sampled ground — a POSITION test that is true and says nothing about winding. Now **249/249 faces up, min normal z 0.99925, mean 0.99999**. The script is also made re-runnable, and its object control now excludes the subject from BOTH snapshots (it was excluded only from `after`, which was right while the script could only create the object and fires spuriously now that it also replaces one).
- **The geometry did not move, and that is the control.** verts/polys/tris 508/249/498 before and after, and the sorted world vertex positions hash identically (`b249072723c55974`). Only winding and COLOR_0 changed.
- **The material fork is retired.** `edging_hardscape` was the only one of 37 MAT_Stone_Trim primitives without COLOR_0, which skipped the StoneAO multiply AND made GLTFLoader cache a second instance (its key carries `vertex-colors:`). Baked with `bake_ao_raycast.py` at the same 0.40 floor: mean **0.858**, between `fountain_bowl_lip` 0.864 and `finial_plinth` 0.812 — correctly high for a flat strip in the open. All 39 primitives now carry COLOR_0, and the live scene reports **16 material instances on p5k against 17 on p5g and p5h**.
- **THE CLEARANCE DID NOT NEED CORRECTING, AND THE MEASUREMENT SAYS SO.** The brief asked to raise the 30 mm lift above the ground's Draco quantum. The quantum is real — **31.74 mm**, confirmed on the DECODED mesh where `ground_plane`'s 15,455 vertices carry only 218 distinct Y values exactly that far apart — but the clearance that survives is what matters, so all **568** edging vertices were ray-cast against the shipped post-Draco ground: **min 17.71 mm, median 18.98, max 50.72, ZERO vertices at or below the ground, zero under 5 mm**, against ~0.54 mm of depth precision at 30 m. Roughly 33 depth units at the thinnest. Raising it would also cost something real — this ribbon has no side wall, so more lift does not make a taller kerb, it floats a plane over the grass. **Left at 30 mm, on evidence.**
- **Graft was a strict REPLACE with NO growth waiver needed:** replaced 1, added 0; transform, POSITION bounds, vertex count and index count all matched unaided. nodes 483 → 483, triangles 186,007 → 186,007, materials 17 → 17, textures 40 → 40, images 40 → 40. The `meshes` +1 and +4 KB are the superseded mesh: the graft never removes bufferViews, which is what lets it prove the survivors byte-identical. **Pre-existing methodology** — p5a already carries 1 orphan (25 KB), p5g/p5h carry 19 (82.8 KB), p5k makes it 20 (84.9 KB, 0.5% of the file).
- **P5C correction ACCEPTED on four pose-matched cameras** (p5k 0.0000/0.0073/0.0076/0.0000 against p5h 0.0000/0.0073/0.0075/0.0000). Edging coverage **0.00% → 0.72 / 0.69 / 0.69 / 0.72%**, L **129.11 / 127.34 / 117.77 / 144.17**. Hierarchy at HERO: masonry 133.02 > **edging 129.11** > terrace 116.23 > steps 109.89 > urn 100.85 ≈ mansion 100.44 > drive 94.91 > lawn 70.71 > hedge 53.55 > cypress 44.86. **Every unrelated class ≤ 0.19 luma**; the single largest move anywhere was WEST `steps` at 0.41, a ~900-px class that does not even use MAT_Stone_Trim and therefore bounds the instrument's noise rather than the change. No z-fighting, no float and no aliasing beyond the terrace's own edge, verified at 5× on the thinnest, farthest section. **Cost zero** — no new triangles, textures, materials, draw calls or GPU residency, and one FEWER material instance.
- **The fair objection, and why it did not carry.** The edging is the second-brightest class BY MEAN, which is exactly the "artificial outline" the brief warns about. But mean rank ignores area: by area-weighted light contribution (L × coverage) it is **93** against terrain 3045, mansion 850, terrace 714, roof 384, drive 309 and masonry 263. It is bright but small, it reads as a stone margin at all four cameras, and it finally delivers what P5C was authored for — the terrace previously met grass on a bare line. Reservation recorded: the band is flat and low-detail at distance, and it raises the largest ground-level luminance step from 45 to 58.
- **CYPRESS NORMAL 512² — TESTED AND ACCEPTED.** Not the rejected P5J experiment: that changed the CODEC (ETC1S, −25.5 luma at NW); this changes RESOLUTION and keeps UASTC. Predicted free from sampling: the map is a 1.5 m tile at 1024² = 1.46 mm/texel, the cypresses are never nearer than ~40 m where NW resolves ~43 mm/px — a **30× minification**, so the GPU samples around mip 5 (32×32), and a 512² map's mip 4 is that same level from the same box filter. The base being deleted is one no camera reaches. **Measured: cypress identical to two decimals in mean AND standard deviation at all four cameras** (HERO 44.86/15.56, WEST 56.72/17.27→17.29, NW 65.75/14.95, dusk 38.29/12.16→12.17), coverage identical.

- **Benefit:** GPU texture residency **62.65 → 61.65 MB (−1.00 MB)**, GLB **16.625 → 15.921 MB (−0.70 MB wire)**, cypress normal 990.22 KB → 269.62 KB (**−72%**). `patch_material_textures.py` gained a per-slot `"maxpx"` key for it, beside the existing `"codec"`; controls keep the 1024 default because their job is to reproduce the SHIPPED image byte-for-byte.

One cumulative decision — p5m sits on p4e, which had never been promoted, so this ships the whole Phase 4 surface system with Phase 5. **A trap avoided:** `MODEL_CANDIDATES.v5` was bound to `EXTERIOR_MODEL_URL`, so changing the constant would have silently re-pointed the `?model=v5` rollback at the promotion itself; `v5` is now its own literal path. Verified on the DEFAULT route with no `?model=` at all: the GLB actually requested is `exterior_mansion_v6_p5m.glb`, 483 meshes, **16 material instances**, 9 lights / 1 caster, all four poses `onBeat`, **0 console errors**, `/hall` still on `interior_hall.glb`.

The MEASUREMENTS were unaffected (readPixels reads the WebGL back buffer; terrain still read 70.53 against 70.52 on a clean run), but the captures were unusable for visual judgement. The probe now dismisses it with "Essential only" — the privacy-preserving option, and analytics have no business in a measurement run. Earlier passes only escaped this because a cookie happened to persist.

## [2026-09-08 12:15 IST] Phase 6 / part 1: the presentation layer's missing contracts, and a 42.7 MB texture defect

- **The forensic pass found far more built than the brief assumes.** `WorldCanvas` + `journey.ts` + `cameraPath.ts` + `interiorPath.ts` already drive one continuous exterior→interior film; `places.ts` is a real 7-place/14-surface node registry with 9 tests; the preloader reads three's own `LoadingManager` and waits for `active` rather than 100%; constellation, embers, selective bloom, turntables with pointer capture, holograms bound to published projects and a WebGL fallback all exist and work. Mobile 390, tablet 768, desktop 1440 and reduced-motion all boot with **0 console errors**, scroll un-trapped, no horizontal overflow. Phase 6 was therefore an audit-and-finish job, and that framing decided everything below.
- **Doc-vs-code discrepancies recorded, code wins.** Fonts are Inter + Playfair, not Fraunces. There is no seven-node camera flight — the implemented model is 7 places / 14 surfaces, and `places.test.ts` explicitly defends it ("If this ever inverts, the design has drifted back into a camera flight per page"). `/projects`, `/book-a-site-visit` and `/sitemap` are registered but 404 — already excluded in `sitemap.ts` under `UNBUILT`, and **nothing links to them**, verified by grepping every internal href.
- **THE INTERIOR LIGHTMAP WAS UPLOADED FOUR TIMES.** `interior_hall.glb` carries ONE lightmap image (1,472 KB on the wire), ONE `textures[]` entry, SEVEN materials. At runtime: seven `THREE.Texture` objects, 4096² with 13 mips, 21,845 KB each, sharing exactly **one ArrayBuffer and one image**, with **four already holding their own `__webglTexture`**. Not an asset or loader defect — GLTFLoader clones a texture whenever a material asks for a non-zero `texCoord` (six of the seven declare `texCoord: 1`), the clone shares its payload, and `WebGLProperties` keys on the object. `shareDuplicateTextures` merges only textures agreeing on image identity AND every property that could change a fetched texel — channel, colour space, wrapping, filtering, anisotropy, flipY, offset/repeat/rotation — so it cannot alter a pixel; `envMap` is excluded because it is app-assigned and shared with the exterior. Orphans are **disposed**, not dereferenced: three frees a GL texture only on an explicit `dispose()`. **7→2 objects, 4→2 uploads, −42.7 MB resident; referenced payload 292.85 → 170.19 MB.** Proven identical on three time-pinned runs: the largest interior difference is **eight pixels out of 1.28 million** above 8/255, peak 23; two interior frames differ in zero.
- **The ember field was different on every page load.** `Motes` used unseeded `Math.random()` for 2,400 points, so with the pose identical to three decimals and the clock pinned, two loads of the same build still differed by mean 0.045 / 2,945 px>8 / peak 146. That floor was larger than most changes worth measuring — it hid real differences and manufactured false ones, flagging a hall-only texture change as a regression on an exterior frame. Now mulberry32 with a fixed seed: same distribution, same look, same field every time. Verified by reading ember positions back (identical to 4 dp), not by pixels. `Constellation.tsx` had already made this choice and says so.
- **URL SYNCHRONISATION, AS A FRAGMENT AND NOT A PATH.** Writing `/about` into the bar while the home page's DOM is on screen makes the URL and the document disagree — reload lands elsewhere, a crawler indexes the wrong markup, back restores a state that never existed. `#constellation` is a real linkable address for a moment *inside* the document, costs no request, and degrades to a plain anchor because each chapter section now carries the matching `id`. `replaceState` not `pushState`: nine chapters would otherwise be nine history entries and back would walk the film backwards. It lives in the PAGE, not the canvas, because the canvas is absent under reduced motion and without WebGL and those visitors still scroll. **Measured: 0 document requests and 0 history entries across the walk; deep links landed at scrollY 4363/8771/12570 against section tops 4364/8771/12570.**
- **ONE ROUTE TRANSITION FOR THE WHOLE SITE.** The existing veil covers only the scroll crossover, inside the canvas, on one page. `RouteVeil` is DOM in the `(site)` layout because it must survive navigations that unmount the canvas — `/projects` is outside `(experience)` — and exist on pages that never had one. Holograms use the same device. Every decision biased toward "navigation works": it opens on a watchdog regardless; modifier clicks, `target`, `download`, `/api/`, cross-origin and same-page fragments all fall through untouched; reduced motion skips the dissolve but still navigates client-side. **Tier1→Tier1 keeps the SAME canvas element; 0 document requests on every case; veil closes to opacity 1.00 and always reopens.**
- **A REAL BUG THE VEIL EXPOSED, AND IT WAS MINE.** Clicking a project card from a station chapter left the visitor on `/` with the bar reading `/#station-1` and the veil shut until the watchdog fired — the navigation did not happen. `ChapterUrl`'s 180 ms settle timer fired INSIDE the router's push and `replaceState`'d the address out from under it; the App Router reconciles against `window.history`, so a second writer during a push does not clash, it loses the navigation. Fixed on three signals: a link click to a different path, a committed path change, and `RouteVeil.isNavigating()` — the last covering what a click listener cannot see, a programmatic push from a hologram.
- **A reduced-motion defect caught before it shipped:** the first veil registered its controller only when motion was allowed, leaving `veiledPush` with no router and sending those visitors through `window.location.assign` — a full document load that tears down the WebGL context and re-parses both models.
- **RESUME, AND WHY IT IS NOT ALWAYS THERE.** The chapter is remembered in `sessionStorage` (not local — one visit's position in one film) and offered back in the header only when the visitor is off the film AND this session has reached a chapter. A permanent button would be a fifth item in a header that deliberately carries four, and on a first visit a second home link in costume. Following it lands at scrollY 10038 against station-2's own offsetTop of 10037.
- **THE HEADER IS TRANSLUCENT, WHICH MADE A LAYOUT PLAN BLEED THROUGH IT.** `bg-[#0A1120]/72` with a backdrop blur, so a departing chapter showed THROUGH the bar: 62px of the Lucky Garden plan at scroll 0.78 and 62px of the Gayatri plan at 0.87, full-colour plot drawings behind the logo. Two chapters were also legible at once at 0.60/0.78/0.87 — which site-home's own comment says a full-viewport sticky pane *cannot* do (it can: the pane shows one, the CONTENT overlaps). One fade fixes both. **After: every held chapter at opacity 1.000 (7 of 7), one chapter legible everywhere, 0.000 plan brightness in the band at all three offsets.**
- **A CSS MASK WAS IMPLEMENTED FIRST AND DOES NOT WORK**, recorded because it looks correct and is cheaper. `mask-image` is anchored to the ELEMENT box: a `sticky top-0` pane is pinned at viewport 0 for most of its life so the band lines up, but the moment it unsticks its box top goes negative and the fade band travels off the top of the screen — gone exactly when needed. The capture at 0.78 still showed the plan bleeding through. A scrim cannot do it either: a gradient over the content is also over the canvas, since the world is at z-0 beneath the page at z-10. **And the trigger must come from the CONTENT, not the pane** — the first working version read the pane's rect, whose top is a constant while pinned, and was drawing the constellation and the hall at 0.762 opacity at their held midpoints.
- **THE SLICE-0 TEST SUITE WAS ASSERTING A WORLD THAT NO LONGER EXISTED** and is deleted, not patched: it read `persistence-probe` testids that live only in `ExperienceCanvas.tsx` (a skeleton the app stopped importing when `WorldCanvas` replaced it), asserted headings `/about` and `/why-us` no longer carry, and called a headless rAF count "sustains 60fps on the reference machine". Replaced by `e2e-experience/experience.spec.ts` (20 cases) under its own config — own config because the default seeds fixture data into whatever `DATABASE_URL` points at, and against `next start` because on the dev server a route's first navigation pays for its webpack compile, which tripped the veil's watchdog and made the suite report a veil defect that was a compiler. **NOT YET EXECUTED — authored and typechecked only.**
- **NO MEMORY LEAK, on two laps of the segment-crossing loop with every hop a CLIENT navigation** (a `page.goto` resets heap and renderer, so comparing two fresh loads measures the HTTP cache and nothing about the app). Lap 2 against lap 1: **geometries 0, textures 0, programs 0, canvases 0, heap +9 MB** (against +112 MB for lap 1's one-off cost), one renderer per round trip by design. **2 GLB requests across the whole run** — drei's module-level cache survives the canvas rebuild.
- **THREE MEASUREMENTS WERE WRONG AND WERE DISCARDED RATHER THAN PUBLISHED.** (1) A settled camera is not a settled frame: the first before/after diff was decided entirely by 350 ms of ember drift, because the pre-change run settled at 1695 ms and the two post-change runs at 1327 and 1345 ms — the two "same build" runs agreed more closely with each other than with the third. Captures are now pinned to a deadline with `lateBy` reporting misses and the scene's own phase (ember `uTime`, turntable angle) read at each shutter. (2) `imgdiff.py`'s verdict was a pure ratio against the control, which cannot return "identical" when the control is ~0 — it flagged four frames whose largest difference was eight pixels; it now has an absolute floor. (3) A first boot measurement reported cold 2085 ms and warm 3621 ms; warm cannot be slower than cold from caching, so the sample was measuring something else. Re-measured over six alternating runs: **cold 3145 ms, warm 3144 ms, spread ±6 ms** — on localhost the boot is not network-bound at all, it is Draco decode + KTX2 transcode + GPU upload, and 3145 ms is a floor rather than a visitor's number.
- **Two harness traps, both of which cost runs.** A `webglcontextlost` listener counts zero while the console plainly logs "THREE.WebGLRenderer: Context Lost.", because r3f calls `forceContextLoss()` on a canvas it is already detaching; counting the renderers three announces sees the same thing from the side that can. And the Playwright MCP runner's vm has **no global `URL`** — `new URL(r.url()).pathname` inside a `page.on('request')` handler throws asynchronously and takes the whole connection down, surfacing as "Connection closed" with no error and no stack. That killed three runs and was diagnosed as a timeout twice.
- **Regression:** typecheck **5/5**, **266 tests**, lint **5/5 / 0 errors**, two clean production builds from a removed `.next`, **0 console errors** on every measured route and viewport. Exterior census matches Phase 5's promoted figures exactly (186,007 triangles, 16 material instances, 61.67 MB) — a cross-check that the promotion is what is running.
- Full report: `docs/PHASE6_REPORT.md`. **PHASE 6 NOT COMPLETE** — no city layer, the constellation composition and interior lighting are named and unaddressed, the E2E suite is unrun. Nothing pushed.

## [2026-09-08 23:55 IST] Phase 6B / completion: the film leaves the house, and the frame stops costing 955 draw calls

- **THE E2E SUITE HAD NEVER BEEN RUN, AND RUNNING IT FOUND A CRASH AND TWO DEFECTS.** 17/4 on the first execution. (1) **The DOM scroll track was swallowing every canvas pointer event** — a `z-10` full-viewport layer over a `z-0` canvas, so table drag and hologram click had been unreachable on any pointer device since the track was written. Fixed with `pointer-events-none` plus `[&>*]:pointer-events-auto`, and `!pointer-events-none` on `<main>` because the `!` is needed to beat the descendant rule's specificity. (2) **React #185 on roughly one in four Tier-2 loads.** drei's `useProgress` is a zustand store whose `set()` runs once per LoadingManager item; ~90 Draco/KTX2 dependencies across two GLBs is ~90 synchronous store writes, enough of which land mid-commit to pass React's nested-update limit of 50. Reads are coalesced to one per animation frame — a rAF callback is a fresh task, so the counter resets rather than climbing. 5 errors / 5 walks before, 0 / 5 after, and **NOT a Phase 6 regression**: the same walk against `src` at `ed7e3e7` gave 8 / 5. (3) **The readiness helper was a no-op** — `overflow !== 'hidden'` is already true before the `dynamic(ssr:false)` preloader mounts, so every probe "settled" at ~359 ms with an empty scene while the cover lifts at ~2870.
- **THE CONSTELLATION CHAPTER HAD NO SCENE IN IT, AND THAT IS A DRAW-CALL COUNT.** Photographed on the shipped build: `mansion_walls` coverage **0.000** from scroll 0.285 onward, **4 draw calls** against 955 at the hero, sky share **64.4 %**. The camera turned 180 degrees off the building to face a sphere parked 46 m out in open field, so every piece of authored geometry spent the last third of the exterior film behind the lens and a stock equirect meadow became the subject by default. The sphere is back above the spire — which is where CLAUDE.md put it: "at the top the house there will be a pointed end ... and at the top there will be a glowing ball". Clearance is derived, not eyeballed: the hover shader pushes points out by 1.18 m at radius 6.2, so the lowest a point can ever reach is 12.42 against a spire tip at 11.72.
- **THE LAST TWO BEATS ARE A CRANE, NOT A TURN AWAY**, and the composition was argued in numbers before it cost a rebuild. New tool `tools/capture/frame_solve.mjs` projects the measured estate bounds through a candidate pose: mansion box [497, 437, 1156, 919] (46 % of frame width), constellation centre [821, 255] at 39 % of frame height, left third clear to x 497. Beats 0.0, 0.3 and 0.58 are untouched — those are the vantages Phase 5 approved.
- **EVENING FALLS ACROSS THE LAST EXTERIOR CHAPTER, and it is not a mood dial.** The exterior ships a daylight grade; the room it cuts to is candlelit, with sconce flames and a lightmap baked for lamplight. The film already crossed from noon to night and the veil was hiding the cut rather than motivating it. It also fixes the constellation for free: **additive light over a sunlit equirect sky is invisible by construction**, which is why a 12 m sphere over a cloud bank read as dust. Every term is identity at evening 0, so the hero is unchanged — and the diff says so: against a same-build control differing by 1,289 px>8, the before/after hero differs by 2,552 with **zero differing pixels above row 300**, i.e. none in the sky, where a lighting change would show first. Sky share 64.4 % → 45.9 %, sky mean 111.8 → 32.2, peak-over-sky contrast 1.67× → **6.5×**.
- **THREE VALUES WERE ALL AUTHORED TO ARRIVE AT THE END OF A LEG, WHICH IS BEHIND A VEIL OR UNDER A FOOTER.** The constellation's reveal reached 1.0 at legProgress 0.92 and the crossover veil starts closing at 0.922 — measured, reveal read **0.215** at the chapter's opening and **0.726** at its MIDPOINT, so the object the chapter is named after was never once seen at strength in a held frame. The evening ramp had the same shape and photographed at 0.72 where the fallen frame reads 58.7. The city chapter's reveal had it a third time. All three now complete inside their chapter.
- **A 2.17× PARAMETER ERROR THAT HAD BEEN UNREACHABLE.** `ExteriorLighting` read `atmosphereAt(DOCUMENT scroll)` while the camera reads the EXTERIOR LEG — at the constellation beat, where legProgress is 1.0, it was sampling 0.42 and returning the values authored for the revolution. It went unseen because the daylight branch returned before it, and daylight is what ships.
- **THE INTERIOR ESTABLISHING SHOT WAS NOT "FLAT" IN THE WAY THE REPORT GUESSED.** Measured, it had no clipping and a usable range — mean 57.6, p05 13.8, p95 120.6. Range is not structure. What it lacked was a subject: left wall 99.8, balustrade 89.5, right wall 80.3, **portrait 56.2**, floor 26.3. The brightest thing in the shot was the wallpaper, and the founder's portrait rendered darker than the plaster it hangs on. The beat moves 2.35 m right and 1.05 m back so both walls converge; **it deliberately does NOT get a station into frame** — the room is 15 × 11 m and from any vantage holding the stair, S1 sits 54 degrees off axis against a 40-degree half-angle, so fitting both needs an 80-degree lens.
- **`distance` IS NOT A SOFT BOUND IN THREE, AND THAT COST A REBUILD.** It is a windowing term `pow(1 - d/cutoff, decay)` multiplied on top of the inverse square, so a picture light with a 5 m cutoff lost 62 % of its output at a portrait 1.9 m away and did almost nothing at intensity 9. At distance 12 / intensity 40 the portrait's face goes 73.1 → **126.2** while the plaster beside it moves 106.2 → 108.0. The hierarchy is now portrait 126 > plaster 108 > stair 61 > floor 22.
- **A THIRD OF THE HALL CARRIES NO LIGHTMAP.** MAT_Trim_Cream 216 primitives with 0 UV1 (the anthemion frieze, the capitals, every ornament), MAT_Gold 46, MAT_Wood_Dark 18, MAT_MarbleFloor 2, the rug 2 — lit by `ambientLight 0.12` and nothing else. `envMapIntensity` is per material, so raising it reaches exactly those surfaces and cannot touch a lightmapped one; the metals needed it most, because an unlit metal reflects nothing and is simply black. Gold 83.9 → 103.5, and the change touches 8,400 pixels confined to the sconce arms, the frieze, the picture light and the newels.
- **TWO BLACK SLABS THAT WERE NOT A DEFECT.** Three explanations fitted the picture, so new tool `tools/capture/whatis.mjs` raycasts named frame pixels instead: `wallpanel_-1_12` and `wallpanel_-1_36`, MAT_Wood_Dark, `lightMap` false, with a real base-colour map — authored dark-wood dado panels, twelve of them around the room. **Left alone.** Its first implementation compared projected bounding boxes and was useless, because a mesh straddling the camera projects to a box thousands of pixels wide.
- **THE HOLOGRAM WAS EMITTING THE SHEET'S PAPER.** It was never flat for want of geometry — `HOLO_S1` ships 9,960 vertices of extruded plot volumes. Measured on the three sanctioned plan textures, **84.8 %, 89.3 % and 76.4 % of pixels are transparent**: the artist masked the paper out in alpha, and nothing read that mask, because glTF declares these materials OPAQUE and the alpha reaching an additive blend comes from the BASE colour, never from an emissive map. So every plate emitted the brightest and largest part of the sheet. Keying on `emissiveMap.a` — the authored mask, not a heuristic — leaves every line exactly where the sanctioned sheet puts it and stops the paper it was drawn on from emitting light.
- **AND THE BLOCKS WERE ADDITIVE OVERDRAW, NOT AN EXPOSURE PROBLEM.** From station 2's lower vantage a hundred plot volumes each contributing a fifth of a unit summed to a flat white sheet. Solid volumes fix it structurally: they write depth, so a pixel is one block instead of fifteen, they occlude each other the way a physical site model does, and the plate underneath is correctly hidden. Cost went DOWN — 72/66/81 draw calls → 70/64/79, 23,752/21,328/33,840 triangles → 17,750/15,458/25,652.
- **TWO SELF-INFLICTED DEFECTS ON THE WAY OUT, BOTH RECORDED BECAUSE BOTH IMPERSONATED THE THING THEY WERE NOT.** The unbaked-environment lift reached the holograms too — they arrive at metalness 1 / roughness 1 with a black base, which is a rough mirror, so six times the room's environment turned the plan plates into sheets of reflected wall that looked exactly like the overdraw defect they had just stopped being. And keying the compiled shader by ROLE meant every plate shared one program: materials that share a cache key share the compiled GLSL, and whichever compiles first decides what all of them run.
- **THE HALL HAS NO WINDOW, AND NO HOLE EITHER.** Parsed from the GLB: 545 nodes, and the only aperture in the shell is `int_door_arch` with `int_doors` in it at z 5.25. The first implementation dissolved the doors and revealed the plaster behind them — a raycast through the threshold frame returned `int_wall_front` dead centre at 3.69 m. The arch and the doors are decorative panels on the face of a solid wall. So the wall opens: a clone of its material discards fragments inside the doorway's own measured rectangle, widening from the centre line as the chapter arrives. Both materials are cloned for their one mesh — the plaster is on 22 primitives and the dark wood on 18.
- **THERE ARE NO COORDINATES, SO NONE ARE USED.** Queried live: every published project has `centroid` NULL and `bbox` NULL, `geometry_pub` 0 rows, `pois_pub` 0 rows. The field is a DIAGRAM OF THE NETWORK and says so on the page — district, publication order, slug hash, unit count and locality are what it is built from, and an E2E case asserts the sentence "a diagram of the network, not a map" is present. Ten unit tests hold the derivation, including that it still works at 24 projects across 3 districts. **Four draw calls whatever publishes**; measured at the threshold beat the whole frame is 20 calls and 4,554 triangles.
- **TWO FRAMING DEFECTS FOUND BY MEASUREMENT.** The field was first laid out at ±34 m and the doorway frames a cone of ±37.6 degrees, so two of three beacons fell behind the jamb — a beacon system that cannot be seen. And the threshold beat first stood at the throw that holds the whole 3.74 m opening in frame, which produced a keyhole; at 1.75 m back the opening subtends 75 degrees against the frame's 80, so the visitor stands IN the doorway rather than looking at it from across the room.
- **ONE BEACON WAS BEHIND THE CHAPTER'S OWN COPY**, so a click on it landed on the list rather than the scene — for the test and for a visitor alike. Found because the E2E asked `document.elementFromPoint` what was actually under the point it was about to click. The field is now centred 3.5 m toward screen-right and the column narrowed to the measure every other chapter uses.
- **THE DIVE NEEDED THE RIG TO STOP DAMPING IT.** The film's 3.1 s lag is what makes scrolling feel like a move; a 620 ms dive fed through it travelled 9 % of the way before the veil closed — 0.15 m, which is pointer-parallax scale. A dive now runs on a 0.11 s constant and hands over to the existing route veil at 300 ms. It is not a second transition device: a marker and the link beside it end in the same veiled client-side push, with **zero document requests** measured.
- **THE KEYBOARD PATH IS THE LIST, NOT A FOCUSABLE BEACON.** The canvas is aria-hidden, and putting a focusable control inside an aria-hidden subtree is a defect, not an accessibility win. So the list is the interactive layer and the marker is its rendering: focus a project and its marker lifts, press Enter and the route is the same veiled push.
- **955 DRAW CALLS → 233, AND THE CENSUS DECIDED IT.** New tool `tools/capture/geometry_census.mjs` groups every visible mesh by family: **363 of the hero's 481 meshes are static masonry on TWO materials**, and the ashlar blocks carry twenty-two triangles each — roughly 726 of the 955 calls for 64k of the 185k triangles. Merged: 955 → **233** calls, 481 → 120 meshes, triangles **unchanged** at 367,558 because nothing was simplified. Against a same-build control, all six exterior frames are inside the build's own run-to-run repeatability — the ember field moving between runs is a larger difference than the change under test. Deliberately not merged: the fountain, the glass, the cypresses, the terrain, and anything `applyGrade` or a probe looks up by name.
- **THREE E2E FAILURES FIXED AT THEIR CAUSES.** Adding the city chapter renormalised the interior leg from 1.01 to 1.21 and three tests broke, none of them testing the wrong thing. The address-bar walk now reads `journey.chapters()` — the same table ChapterUrl reads — after two wrong derivations, one from hardcoded fractions and one from section offsets, which is a different quantity again because the page carries a held frame, a sold-out block and a footer after the film. The turntable's search fractions are MEASURED by a new sweep tool rather than derived: at a station's beat the camera frames the hologram and the table is out of frame, so the beat is exactly where the proxy is not reachable. And the dive test read the camera once at a fixed offset, which is a race against whichever frame the browser drew; it now samples across the dive window and takes the furthest the camera gets.
- **A CAPTURE MISTAKE THAT PRODUCED A CONVINCING PICTURE OF A DEFECT THAT WAS NOT THERE.** Photographing the city chapter at its SECTION's midpoint showed the copy cut off at the top. It does not overflow: each film section is about 1.2 viewports tall, so its sticky pane pins for roughly a fifth of a viewport and then rides up — the midpoint catches the copy on its way off the screen. Measured properly the pane is 844 px and the content 466. The capture fractions now name the START of the pin window.
- **TWO THIRDS OF THE BEACONS WERE OFF SCREEN ON A PHONE.** The lens is authored as a vertical fov, so at 390×844 the horizontal cone is three and a half times narrower than at the reference aspect. The field now scales its spread by the viewport's aspect; the layout itself is untouched and still deterministic.
- **Final regression:** typecheck **5/5**, lint **5/5 / 0 errors**, **279 unit tests**, clean production build, **E2E 27/27 executed against `next start`**, **0 console errors** across a ten-frame desktop matrix and the mobile and tablet runs, 0 document requests for client-side project navigation.
- Full report: `docs/PHASE6_REPORT.md`, PART TWO. **PHASE 6 COMPLETE.** Limitations named in §6B.28 — the interior establishing frame is now the film's most expensive at 552 calls and is not optimised, the census tool misreports on the interior leg, and the film's chapter fractions and the DOM's section offsets are close but not equal. Nothing pushed.
