## [2026-09-14 23:30 IST] Phase 3 / completion: the architecture authored on 1 September reaches production - and 52 of its 56 pieces were inside out

- **PHASE 3 WAS NEVER IN PRODUCTION.** `352dd3b` closed it with production left on v5, and Phase 2.5B, 4 and 5 all branched from the 27 August source instead of P3.4. Counted in the shipped files: p34 has 8 `P3_` nodes and 50 quoin-named nodes; p25b3, p4e and **p5m have 0 and 2** (the rustic base's kit). The ashlar ran 5.07 m to the cornice with bare wall at every corner, the columns met their plinths on a bare cut, the door met raw wall. Finished as **`p3f`**: p5m plus the P3.4 set - column bases, door jambs, a third tread, the crowning cornice, the portico bed mould, the architrave fillet and 48 corner quoins - and **promoted**. p5m stays addressable as `?model=p5m`.
- **p34 COULD NOT BE GRAFTED AS IT WAS, FOR FOUR MEASURED REASONS.** (1) The quoins carried flat StoneAO 1.0 and no `p4_blocktone`, where every ashlar block carries a P4A tone. (2) The trim pieces carried one hand-matched AO constant each (0.451-0.823), where every production trim surface carries a raycast bake. (3) **52 of the 56 pieces are inside out** - the fillet, both jambs, the tread and all 48 quoins are closed boxes with a NEGATIVE signed volume, every triangle flipped by recalc. three culls back faces, so each drew its own far inner walls from outside: quoins read recessed, the tread as a dark slab past the cheek walls, and the AO bake sent every ray into the piece, putting three of them on the 0.40 floor. The column bases and crown are correct; the bed mould's two reversed triangles are the far fan of each ~5 cm2 end cap over its cyma section and are left. (4) Encoding - see the next bullet.
- **A SILENT DRAW-CALL REGRESSION WAS WAITING FOR THE FIRST QUOIN.** p5m's 267 ashlar blocks carry COLOR_0 as UNSIGNED_SHORT normalised VEC4 (via P4A's transplant); `export_web.py`, the donor path for everything else, writes FLOAT VEC3. The runtime merge grouped MAT_Stone_Wall by material and attribute NAME, and three's `mergeGeometries` - tested with this repo's own three - returns **null** on inconsistent array types, which skips the whole group. One grafted quoin would have put every ashlar block back on its own draw call and undone Phase 6B's 955 -> 233 with only a console.error to say so. The merge key now carries each attribute's array type, item size and normalisation; the quoins merge into their own batch (**merged 3, meshes removed 411**).
- **RE-AUTHORED IN THE PRODUCTION SCENE, NOT COPIED.** New `tools/blender/p3f_integrate.py` appends the 56 objects into P5K (never writing it), folds the `MAT_Stone_*.001` copies onto the Phase 4 materials and removes only what the append brought (3 materials, 9 pre-Phase-4 images), turns the 52 inverted pieces right way out, asserts the world-box UV rule (max error 0.0), rebuilds StoneAO as one FLOAT_COLOR layer, tones the quoins with blocktone's distribution (mean 0.9941 / sd 0.0485 against the ashlar's 0.9991 / 0.0447), and bakes the pieces with the ARCH occluder set - fillet now mean 0.784, tread 0.580, jambs 0.50, instead of the floor.
- **NEIGHBOURS WERE MEASURED BEFORE ANY WERE TOUCHED.** Every exposed sample within 1.6 m baked with the pieces hidden and present. Rule: re-bake an object if any sample moves by more than 0.05. That selects `lion_frieze` (2,619 samples, worst -0.45 where the bed mould closes the step), `mansion_walls` (307, -0.34 at the column foot), both entry cheeks and `entry_step_0`, and leaves the portico architrave, cornice and cymatium (<= 0.035), the urns and the terraces untouched. A bake WITHOUT the pieces reproduces what production ships to a mean of 0.0000-0.0034, so the re-bake changes what Phase 3 causes and nothing else. `entry_step_1` is excluded on purpose: its shipped AO does not reproduce (the P5H urns went in beside it without a re-bake) and Phase 3 moves it by 0.001.
- **AND WHAT THE RE-BAKE MUST NOT SHIP, PUT BACK.** `p3f_neighbours.py` restores two classes of loop to their shipped values: 6 `mansion_walls` loops more than 5 m from any piece that moved by <= 0.019 - P5C's ground edging, Phase 5's staleness, not Phase 3's - and **every loop the new geometry buries**. That second class was found on screen: the column shaft's bottom ring sits inside the new torus, re-baked to the floor, and with no ring between foot and capital it darkened the WHOLE shaft on a straight gradient - **0.743** of its old luminance at the foot, 0.90 at mid-height. Restoring 114 buried loops on the walls and 39 on the frieze takes the foot to **0.938**.
- **THE GRAFT IS THREE STEPS SO EACH WAIVER COVERS ONE NODE, AND EVERY WAIVER IS EXPLAINED BY A COUNT.** A, strict: add 56, replace the cheeks and `entry_step_0`. B: `lion_frieze` 22,379 -> 22,267 vertices on identical 24,117 indices and bounds - the re-bake put 407 more corners on the AO floor and the exporter welds them (source signatures 20,511 -> 20,412). `graft_draco_nodes.py` only admitted a seam re-split in the P5D direction, and routed it through the growth checks besides; it now admits welding too. C: `mansion_walls` with `--allow-growth`, 151,836 -> 151,869 indices, which is exactly the **11 zero-area triangles** the source holds and the 27 August export dropped. Decoded against p5m, every replaced corner lands on a shipped (position, normal, uv); colour changes beyond 1.6 m of a piece: **0** on four objects, 7 of 90,017 on the walls at <= 0.0078.
- **MEASURED.** Hero 233 -> **251** draw calls, 367,558 -> **370,120** drawn triangles, programs and textures unchanged, 0 console errors - two independent probes agree. File 16,694,368 -> 17,644,200 bytes; node-referenced triangles 186,007 -> 187,288 (+1,270, +11 zero-area); 17 materials / 40 textures / 40 images unchanged. Film frames against a same-build control are above noise on hero, revolution and turn, and the changed pixels sit on the corners, the crown line, the entrance and the edges of the shadows those pieces cast. Evidence in `tools/capture/out3f/`.
- **Regression:** typecheck **5/5**, lint **5/5 / 0 errors**, **279 unit tests**, clean production build (33/33), the default route requests `exterior_mansion_v6_p3f.glb`, **E2E 27/27 (11.2m)** against `next start`, memory flat across three canvas teardowns at p5m's steady state plus exactly the 9 new geometries.
- **Residuals, named in §8 of the report:** +492 KB of dead bytes (577 KB total) the graft leaves behind; a <= 6 % darkening still toward each column foot (no vertex ring between foot and capital); the bed mould's two end-cap fan triangles; the tread projects 0.30 m in front of the cheek walls as P3.1 authored it; `blocktone_stoneao.py`'s "deterministic" seed is not (1 of 267 tones reproduced) - shipped tones are safe, a `--reset` re-run is not; Phase 5's stale AO on the east wall base and `entry_step_1` measured and left.
- Full report: `docs/PHASE3_REPORT.md`. **PHASE 3 COMPLETE.**

## [2026-09-18 22:30 IST] Client review, 16 items: the estate rebuilt outside, the hall extended by bays inside, and a nameplate for the portrait

- **THE HALL WAS THE LAST OF THE SIXTEEN, AND IT IS A ROOM AND NOT A SCALE FACTOR.** The review asked for the house to be "bigger, taller, wider, and importantly longer, while preserving the features", inside and out. The exterior answer was a new nine-bay estate (v7, `build_estate_v7.py`); the interior answer could not be, because the hall is a delivered asset with a baked lightmap, a station rig the site binds by node name, and turned joinery the runtime replaces by regex. So it is **extended the way it was built**: `tools/blender/extend_hall_v7.py` re-proportions the shell from **15.0 x 10.6 x 6.4 m to 19.8 x 15.4 x 8.0 m** and adds a bay at each end of every wall — a pilaster order, a panel, a panel moulding and a pair of sconces at y ±7.2 on each side wall (7 bays a side, 12 sconces), a panel bay at each end of the front and back walls, and seven more anthemions per end per wall on the frieze's own 0.320/0.323 m spacing. 663 nodes in the shipped file against 545 before; every node name the runtime knows still matches (`sconce_plate_-1_-7.2` sanitises to `sconce_plate_-1_-72`, which the joinery regex accepts).
- **FOUR THINGS IN THE FIRST DRAFT WOULD HAVE SHOWN ON SCREEN, AND WERE MEASURED OUT BEFORE THE BAKE.** (1) The frieze duplicated at a flat 2.4 m offset, which is not a multiple of either wall's pitch — the first duplicate would have landed 0.16 m from the last original on the front and back walls' 0.320 m spacing and 0.20 m from it on the side walls' 0.3227 m, a half-pitch stutter at each of the eight joins; it now repeats by exactly seven pitches. (2) The back wall's panel mouldings start at x 3.2 and the stair widened to ±3.25, so the moulding's lower corner would have dipped behind the landing; they step out 0.2 m and the new one lands on the same 2.2 m rhythm. (3) Pilasters stretched in z alone would have given the room 25%-taller capitals; the whole order is scaled uniformly about the foot of its wall face instead, and what hangs on it (sconces, their bake lamps) moves the extra 0.12 m out with the face. (4) Stretching the shell kept its UVs, which would have stretched the marble and the plaster with the room; every stretched face's texture coordinates are carried by that face's own world-space UV gradient, so texel density is what it was.
- **THE RE-BAKE IS A NEW NUMBER, NOT A REUSED ONE.** Cycles/OptiX, 4096² atlas, 512 samples, 160 shell objects / 39,471 triangles lightmapped, and the atlas's normalisation divisor came out at **3.0801** against the old room's 4.6597 — surfaces standing further from the same lamps have a lower 99.5th percentile. `HallModel.LIGHTMAP_INTENSITY` and `interior_hall.manifest.json` carry the new figure; shipping the old one would have rendered the room 51% too bright. `bake_lightmap.py` gained an optional save path so the bake of a variant cannot overwrite `mansion_web.blend`, and its normalisation now reads the atlas through `foreach_get` rather than a 67-million-element Python list.
- **THE COLLISION TEST EARNED ITS KEEP AGAIN.** The urns moved out to the foot of the wider stair and grew a fifth with it, which put their tops at y 1.87 — **above** the 1.86 m travelling eye line instead of 14 cm under it. The withdrawal from the last station cut the corner from the station toward the room's centre line, a diagonal that used to pass over an urn and now passed through it: **13 cm inside urn_l leaving S2 and 13 cm inside urn_r leaving S3**, on the 2- and 3-station paths respectively. The beat now leaves along the station's own lane, a metre in toward the room, which is how every other station is already left.
- **EVERY INTERIOR CONSTANT WAS RE-MEASURED FROM THE SHIPPED GLB, NOT SCALED BY HAND.** `interiorPath` (station anchors, lanes, promenade, all twelve beats), `cameraPath.test`'s interior solids and stair surface, `DOORWAY.hallStart/hallPass`, `CityField`'s doorway rectangle (x ±1.54, y < 4.3) and `cityLayout`'s band (26..58, carried out with a doorway that moved 2.4 m), `InteriorStage`'s portrait volume, `WorldCanvas`'s portrait spot, and the five interior poses in `poses.ts`. Checked against the file: doors x ±1.49 y 0..4.26 z 7.57..7.69; stair ±3.25, rising to 3.30 with the landing at 3.47; balustrade to 4.72; portrait frame x ±1.21, y 3.95..7.21, z -7.70..-7.57; chandelier y 5.85..7.85.
- **THE PORTRAIT GOT THE STANDING THE REVIEW ASKED FOR (item 9).** A walnut mount with a gilt fillet behind the frame and an engraved brass plate beneath it, built in `PortraitNameplate.tsx` rather than in the GLB so the wording can change without a re-export and a re-bake. **The name was not invented**: the site publishes the title and the company and not the name, so the plate carried only those two true lines until the client gave the name on 18 September — it now reads **K. BHASKARA RAO** over MANAGING DIRECTOR / QUALITY HOMES REALITY, and setting `FOUNDER.name` back to null returns it to the two-line form rather than to a guess. `portraitNameplate.test.ts` holds it under the frame, above the landing, inside the mount and clear of the back wall's mouldings.
- **ITEM 6 IS DELIBERATELY UNTOUCHED.** "The ball looks cheap — don't touch it now, I will give you the reference of a new ball." Only its world scale and grain followed the larger estate; the geometry and material are exactly as they were, waiting on that reference.
- **ONE E2E ASSERTION WAS STALE TWICE OVER, AND THE SUITE CAUGHT IT.** `the front door > wheeling through the door plays the passage` bounds how close the camera gets while the page is held: `nearestTheDoor < 9`, against a comment describing "the front door plane is z 5.06; the fountain is at 13.2" — the v5 exterior's numbers. v7 puts the door leaves at z 8.17..8.31 and the fountain at 26.05..33.95, and the sample the loop actually lands on is the hall side's own start, which moved from z 8.2 to **10.6** with a room 2.4 m longer. So the run reported 10.6 against a bound of 9. The bound is re-based on the geometry that exists (14) and the comment now says which two numbers it is measuring between; what it still fails on is a passage that leaves the camera parked at the door beat, z 33.6, which is the regression it was written for.
- **Regression:** typecheck clean, lint 0 errors (the two pre-existing warnings only), **121 unit tests** (5 of them new, holding the nameplate under its frame), a clean production build, and the full experience suite **E2E 28/28 (11.2m)** against `next start` — including the crossing into the hall, the table drag, the front-door passage and the district field, each of which asserts zero console errors. Look-dev: all seven interior beats and eleven close-ups captured through the real site, 0 page errors; the room measures mean luma 128-132 against the approved room's 133-137 with no clipping, so it sits in the same exposure band it was graded in.

## [2026-09-19 06:20 IST] "It nowhere looks like a 50L build": the golden hour, the luxury programme, and the lens

- **THE VERDICT WAS ABOUT THE IMAGE, NOT THE MODEL.** "much better than the old one but still any sane person would build it for 60k" — and the honest reading of that is not that the house needs more polygons. It is that nothing had happened to the picture: a noon sun landing flat on every elevation, no environment to reflect, a lawn that was one green, and a framebuffer shown raw with no grade, no grain and no falloff. Every one of those is cheap to fix and each is read instantly.
- **THE GOLDEN HOUR IS THE LARGEST SINGLE CHANGE.** The daylight key moves from [-60, 52, 58] (32 degrees up) to **[-96, 26, 62] (13 degrees)** and from #FFF0DB to **#FFD8A8**, which rakes the south elevation the hero holds and the west flank the new terrace sits on, and throws the palms' shadows the length of the lawn. The shadow frustum widens with it (+/-48 to +/-78, far 240 to 340) because a 13-degree sun casts three times as far, and the map goes to **4096** on high tier, where the palm fronds were coming back as lace. `tools/gltf/make_sky_v7.py` was repainted to match on the same vector: warm horizon on the sun's bearing, cool opposite it, cumulus lit on their sun side, a cirrus deck above. Move one and the other must move.
- **THE EXTERIOR HAD NO ENVIRONMENT MAP AT ALL, AND THAT WAS A BUG.** `RoomEnvironmentMap` mounted for both sets, so outside, every metal, every pane of glass, the fountain and the canal were reflecting a grey studio box at 0.45 — a real part of "it looks like a 3D build", since a reflection is most of what separates a render from a photograph. The sky panorama is now PMREM-convolved and bound as `scene.environment` outside (dusk keeps the room cube: the painted sky is a daylight panorama and an exterior with no environment at all is the defect this removes). A material pass then takes the surfaces that live on reflection up to where they belong — water 2.0-3.0, glass 2.2-2.4, chrome and gilt 1.9-2.2, polished stone 1.5 — and lifts the two surfaces the grade exposed: **the slate roof, which was returning near-black** and is the largest single surface in the hero, and the lawn.
- **THE LUXURY PROGRAMME IS WHAT A HOUSE LIKE THIS IS BOUGHT WITH.** `tools/blender/build_estate_v7.py` grew a `lux` stage: an **infinity pool** on the west terrace (11.4 x 13.2 m, a spill lip at water level over a catch basin, polished travertine deck, glass balustrade, five loungers, two parasols, a cabana, underwater lights and deck bollards), a **helipad** on the north-west lawn (9 m pad, painted ring and H, twelve perimeter lights, a windsock), **three cars** in the motor court, a **stone balustrade round the podium** with gilt urns at its corners, a **formal parterre** in the hero's empty foreground, **flowering beds** at the foot of the house, and uplights and avenue lanterns that the evening channel lights. 4.58 MB to 5.31 MB on the wire.
- **THE CARS WERE REBUILT ONCE, AND THE REASON IS WORTH KEEPING.** The first version stacked boxes and put the wheels at +/-0.86 inside a body +/-0.98 wide, so at forty metres it was a black slab on a drive. A car in silhouette is one SIDE PROFILE extruded across the body, a narrower glasshouse on top of it, and wheels standing proud of both — nothing else at that distance reads as a car.
- **ONE PALM WAS DELETED BY MEASUREMENT.** The reframed hero put a 16.5 m royal palm at Blender (-17, -34.5) **24 m from the lens and 20 degrees off its axis**, laying a frond across the facade in the one frame the whole page is judged on. Checked against `estateBounds` rather than by eye: it was the only planting inside the hero cone, and the forecourt ring now skips that arc.
- **THE HERO WAS RE-FRAMED, AND IT IS A COMPOSITION FIX.** From 15.5 m over a 4.1 m aim the camera looked down onto the roof with the tree belt closing the top of frame: no sky, and the parapet, cupola and spire — the three most expensive lines on the building — read against foliage of their own value. 12.2 m over a 5.0 m aim at 31 m out puts the roofline against the sky, brings the fountain in as a foreground, and keeps the type's gutter to the pixel (azimuth, lens and frame offset untouched).
- **AND THE LENS. `PostFX` gained a grade** — contrast +0.055, saturation +0.09, a sub-pixel radial chromatic aberration and film grain in OVERLAY at 2.8%, all depth-free, which is the constraint this file's own investigation established (anything that asks the composer for a DepthTexture renders from garbage here). The grain also does a technical job: it breaks the banding a smooth sky gradient shows on an 8-bit display. `CinemaOverlay.tsx` adds what belongs in the DOM rather than the scene: the same grain over the whole frame, a warm lens leak on the sun's own bearing, and a hairline gold frame with corner ticks. Aberration and grain are high-tier only; mid keeps bloom, grade and vignette.
- **THE LAWN WAS 45% OF THE HERO AND ONE COLOUR.** The texture now carries four 1.5 m mowing bands per 6 m tile at three times the contrast, warmer and a shade lighter (104,132,64 against 92,124,62), with the stripe in the normal and the roughness as well — a mown lawn is not a colour, it is bands of blades laid toward and away from the mower.
- **Regression:** typecheck clean, lint 0 errors, **121 unit tests** (the camera collision suite re-run against the regenerated `estateBounds` — 163 architecture solids and 472 planting, up from 438 with the new beds — and against the reframed hero), a clean production build, and the full experience suite **E2E 28/28 (11.8m)** against `next start`. Measured at the hero, 1440x900: mean luma 66.0 before the pass and **93.1** after, p95 202.7, 0.09% clipped — brighter AND with more range, which is what a directional key buys over a flat one. The GLB is 4.58 MB -> 5.31 MB. One flake seen and chased down: the door-passage test failed once on `peak` (the white never reported coverage) in a run under load and passed in isolation in 1.4m, then passed again in the clean full run.

## [2026-09-21 18:30 IST] The art-direction review, exterior: baked occlusion, a sky that lights from the lawn, a filmic print, a lens — and two defects that had switched the sun's shadows off

- **THE REVIEW, AND WHAT THIS PASS TOOK FROM IT.** Quality Homes Reality's agent listed five things that make the site read as "a 3D build": flat lighting and synthetic materials, the bracketed navigation, the glowing door transition, the particle dome, and the clip-art logo. The client then struck the dome and the door from the list, so both are untouched, and the logo is a brand decision that is recommended, not made. In scope: the brackets and the frame (done at the start of the pass), and the exterior's light — "HDRI and baked ambient occlusion", "long, soft shadows", "deep shadows, bright highlights", "moody, rich, deep greens rather than neon", "a slight depth-of-field blur on the background trees".
- **THE SUN HAD NO SHADOWS ON EVERY MACHINE FAST ENOUGH TO REACH THE HIGH TIER, AND HAD NOT SINCE THE GOLDEN-HOUR PASS.** That pass tiered the shadow map (1024/2048/4096), and the tier starts provisional at `mid` and is promoted after the first frames are timed. r3f writes the new `mapSize`; three only allocates a shadow map when `shadow.map` is null, so the 2048 texture stayed — and was drawn through a 4096 VIEWPORT, which three sizes from `mapSize`. The map held the lower-left quarter of the sun's view at twice the scale, every receiver looked up somebody else's texel, and nothing on the estate cast a shadow. Found with a `?debug=1` probe that reads the live light: `mapSize [4096,4096]`, `map [2048,2048]`, and the only depths in it were the tree belt under the light. `ExteriorLighting` now disposes a map whose size no longer matches. **This is the single largest change in the pass: the golden hour finally has its long shadows.**
- **THE EXTERIOR HAD NO TONE CURVE, AND THE CODE SAID IT DID.** Since three r153 a material only tone-maps when it renders to the SCREEN; the composer renders into its own buffer and postprocessing's `EffectMaterial` is `toneMapped: false`. So with `<PostFX>` mounted the frame went out as clamped scene-linear — hard-clipped highlights, no toe — and `ColorPipeline`'s exposure was inert. `PostFX`'s header claimed the opposite; it is corrected, and so is the note on `ColorPipeline`. Outside in daylight the curve now comes from `FilmGrade.tsx`: three's ACES fit with its own exposure (so the doorway's `toneMappingExposure` lift, which the review asked to leave alone, stays exactly as inert as it was), then the green grade, a teal/amber split and a gentle S. The hall and the dusk rollback keep the chain they were measured against.
- **"REDUCE THE SATURATION OF THE GREEN GRASS AND TREES."** In the print, not in the assets: hues 60-175 degrees, weighted by their own saturation so stone never enters the band, lose 28% of their chroma, turn 4 degrees away from yellow and drop 10% in value. Limestone (35-45) and sky (210) sit outside it. The pool, which then became the loudest colour in every frame, is taken down to a deep teal.
- **BAKED AMBIENT OCCLUSION, TWO WAYS, BECAUSE THE ESTATE IS TWO KINDS OF SURFACE.** New `tools/blender/bake_estate_ao_v7.py`, now a step of `ship_estate_v7.sh`. The ARCHITECTURE (walls, bands, quoins, pediments, roof, cupola, spire, steps, podium, terraces, fountain, canal, coping) shares one 4096 atlas on UV2 with a Cycles AO bake at a 6 m reach, and the ground is hidden from those rays — three's hemisphere light already models the lower half of a wall seeing the lawn, and counting it again is naive AO's grey band round every building. The GROUND (lawn, drive, gravel, beds, podium top, helipad) is one top-down 4096 map over 216 m, baked as sky irradiance under a white world, SELECTED-TO-ACTIVE from a probe quad under the estate so each texel takes the top-most ground surface: the path tracer sees through alpha-cut leaves, so a rain tree leaves dappled shade rather than an opaque disc. glTF's occlusion slot carries both; three applies `aoMap` to indirect light only, so the sun keeps its shadow map and the sky now has its occlusion. Materials are duplicated (`_AO`, `_AOG`) so a shared material never samples another object's atlas; the runtime strips the suffix for name lookups.
- **THE ATLAS TOOK FOUR TRIES, AND EACH ONE IS NOW A COMMENT IN THE SCRIPT.** No World in the file (the web brings its own sky) left Cycles' AO reach unbounded and baked the whole house grey. 105k faces of balusters and carved trim smart-projected into tens of thousands of slivers whose margins filled 95% of the square (walls at 18 cm a texel) — the atlas is for surfaces, not ornament. Smart projection turned every band, cornice and slab into a hollow ring the shape of the plan — seams on horizontal faces and at 40 degrees fixed that. And the undersides nobody sees, the podium top under the terrace plate, the inside faces of the 60 cm walls and the roof's underside are parked on a reserved white strip. Result: **33% of the square in islands, ~2.8 cm a texel on the house.**
- **THE POOL SHOWED THE LAWN.** The ground grid ran straight under the pool terrace, 20 mm below the water line and 1.5 m above the floor, so through water at 45% alpha the pool was grass. The grid now leaves out every cell wholly inside the terrace footprint; the slab covers all of them except the pool opening. (Also found: `ship_estate_v7.sh` built stages `arch land` and would have silently dropped the pool, helipad and cars on any full rebuild — it now builds `arch land lux`.)
- **THE SKY THE ESTATE IS LIT BY IS NOT THE SKY IT IS SEEN AGAINST.** The painted panorama carries bright haze below its horizon — fine as a backdrop nobody sees, wrong as light: every soffit, cornice underside and shaded elevation was lit from below by a glow as bright as the sky. `make_sky_v7.py` now writes a 1024 x 512 lighting copy with the lawn's bounce below the horizon (a sunlit olive at about a third of the sky's radiance), and `SkyBackground` PMREMs that one.
- **THE REFLECTIVE MATERIALS HAD NEVER HAD THEIR GAINS.** three r173 overwrites `envMapIntensity` with `scene.environmentIntensity` for every standard material that takes its environment from the scene, so the water's 3.0, the glass's 2.2 and the gilt's 1.9 were all the global 0.25. The polished materials now hold the environment explicitly and their gains apply relative to the global.
- **CONTACT-HARDENING SUN SHADOWS.** `softSunShadows.ts` patches three's `getShadow` to PCSS at module load, before anything compiles (drei's `<SoftShadows>` does the same by disposing every material in the scene and truncating three's program cache). The penumbra is the sun's own 0.53 degree disc across the key's shadow camera — now one exported constant both the light and the model read — softened by half: sharp where a column meets the terrace, ~40 cm at the end of a palm's shadow. Reads are explicit-LOD and the result leaves through getShadow's own `return mix(...)` rather than an early return, so ANGLE's D3D compiler has nothing to warn about: 0 X3595 and 0 X4000 lines across a seven-beat capture, where the first build logged one per shadow-receiving program.
- **DEPTH OF FIELD WITHOUT A DEPTH TEXTURE.** The bisect in `PostFX` still stands: asking the composer for a DepthTexture renders from garbage. `LensFocus.tsx` instead has every estate material write its view depth into the half-float colour buffer's alpha (one gated line appended to `opaque_fragment`) and reads it in its own FIRST pass; every EffectPass clamps alpha on its way out, so the distances never reach the canvas. Background only: the focal plane is set each frame 6 m behind the FARTHEST corner of the house's own bounding box (estateBounds) along the camera axis, so no angle the film takes can put a cornice in the blur — `exteriorLens.test.ts` walks the whole exterior leg to hold it — and blur is full 108 m further on, where the tree belt stands; 4 CSS px at most, and a tap only counts if its own blur reaches the pixel, so the house's silhouette never smears. High tier only.
- **AND THE COMPOSER HAD BEEN RUNNING ITS PASSES OUT OF ORDER.** r3f appends an effect that mounts later — the tier promotion again — to the END of the composer's children, so the high-tier chain was Render -> [Bloom, grade, Vignette] -> Aberration -> Grain, and with the lens, Lens after the pass that clamps alpha: it read 1.0 everywhere and blurred the whole frame as sky. Found with `?lensdebug=1`, which paints the depth the lens receives. The composer is now keyed on the tier and rebuilds in written order when the tier is promoted (once, at load). The first build of the lens also negated `vViewPosition.z` (three's is already negated); both are pinned in `exteriorLens.test.ts`.
- **THE THRESHOLD IS A UNIFORM WRITE, NOT A NEW CHAIN.** The set flips from exterior to interior in the middle of the door passage. A chain whose effects depended on the set would rebuild and recompile its passes right there, and the r3f effect wrappers put props into constructor args, so even a changed prop re-instantiates. So every effect either set needs stays mounted for the life of a tier, and the flip only writes uniforms: the print's `amount` (0 is an exact passthrough), the old contrast/saturation pair to its graded values or identity, the vignette's pair, the lens's strength. The hall's output is unchanged.
- **ONE E2E CASE FAILS, AND IT FAILS ON THE COMMITTED BUILD TOO.** `the front door > wheeling through the door plays the passage` reads the white layer's peak coverage and got 0. Reproduced and profiled over CDP: **2,393 ms in `getProgramInfoLog`** inside the first render after the swap, where **35 hall programs** compile synchronously (the hall is mounted early but hidden, so three never compiles it before it is visible), and the test's samples straddle the freeze. The same test on a clean build of `2d82855` (this pass's files stashed) failed **4 of 5** isolated runs on this machine; this build failed 5 of 5. The previous pass logged it as a one-off flake; it is a real ~2.4 s stall in the middle of the door transition. It is left alone here because the client asked for the door transition to stay as it is, and flagged as its own task (pre-compile the hall with its own light rig once it is armed).
- **THE DUSK BEATS OPEN UP.** With a real toe the last exterior chapter fell to a frame mean of 30. The print's exposure now rides the film's own evening factor (+55% at full evening), the way an operator rides the iris through a dusk shot.
- **LOOK-DEV SWITCHES, ALL QUERY-GATED AND INERT WITHOUT THE FLAG:** `?debug=1` publishes the renderer, scene and composer on `window` for probes that read GPU-side state; `?lensdebug=1` paints the depth the lens receives; `?pcss=0` keeps three's PCF for an A/B. They join `?free=1` and `?grade=dusk`.
- **MEASURED** (1440 x 900 at dpr 2, production build, high tier): the committed build (`2d82855`, this pass's files stashed) and this one, captured the same way at the same beats: hero mean luma **100.6 -> 98.6**, p05 **30.9 -> 15.5**, p95 **203.1 -> 202.9**, **0.00% clipped** on both — the same exposure with the shadows twice as deep, p95/p05 **6.6 -> 13.1**; the orbit beat p05 33.2 -> 15.9; the dusk beat 43.4 -> 44.9 in mean, where without the evening lift the new toe took it to 30. Side by side: `C:/dev/Blender/estate_before_after.jpg`. Steady state is vsync-bound (99% of frames at 16.7 ms) with PCSS and the lens on. The GLB is **5.33 -> 8.28 MB**: the two AO maps are 1.69 MB and 1.08 MB as ETC1S, the rest is UV2. The ground bake takes ~75 s and the atlas ~110 s on a laptop GPU (OptiX); the whole `bake_estate_ao_v7.py` step is ~5 minutes.
- **Regression:** typecheck clean, lint 0 errors (the one pre-existing warning), **127 unit tests** (6 new in `exteriorLens.test.ts`: the PCSS patch's placement and uniqueness, the penumbra derived from the key's own shadow camera, the positive-depth write, and the lens never reaching the house anywhere on the exterior leg), a clean production build, and **E2E 27/28 (13.5m)** against `next start` — the one failure is the door passage above, which fails 4 of 5 on the committed build. 0 page errors and 0 shader-compiler warnings across a seven-beat capture. Nothing committed.

## [2026-09-22..23 IST] Old-money pass / the hall: its own reflections, a clean bake, a print, walnut, marble slabs, and stations that are pieces

- **THE BRIEF.** Budget to $100K+; "rich ivory, elegant, old money"; keep the storyboard and the mansion, upgrade the rest. The hall was measured against the approved Cycles renders (FINAL/v8_*) and read milky, flat and game-like. Five causes, each fixed at source.
- **1. NONE OF THE ROOM'S REFLECTION GAINS EVER APPLIED.** three r173 replaces a material's own `envMapIntensity` with `scene.environmentIntensity` whenever the material has no envMap of its own (WebGLRenderer.setProgram). Walnut x9, gilt x9, marble x2.6, the unbaked ornament x6, the nameplate's 6/9/7: all rendered at the global 0.3, against RoomEnvironment — a generic grey studio whose six light panels were the round white blotches on every walnut panel.
- **THE HALL NOW REFLECTS ITSELF** (hallProbe.ts). On the first frame the hall is on screen a cube camera photographs the lit room from its centre (256 px half-float), PMREM prefilters it, and every hall material gets it EXPLICITLY, so per-material gains (PROBE_GAIN) are honoured. Reflections are BOX-PROJECTED onto the room's inner faces (x ±9.9, y 0..8, z ±7.7), a ShaderChunk edit gated on `HALL_PROBE`. Programs compile once: a 256-face RoomEnvironment stand-in is bound first, and swapping it for the capture changes no program parameter. The capture renders only HALL_LAYER, with every light enabled on it for the six renders, so its light state — and every program — is the main render's.
- **2. THE ROOM WAS LIT TWICE.** The lightmap already holds every bounce, but a 0.4 AmbientLight and the environment's diffuse irradiance were added on top of it on every wall: a flat second copy of the room's light, which is exactly a milky veil and lifted corners. Lightmapped hall surfaces now take DIFFUSE light from the bake alone (no ambient, no probe irradiance) and SPECULAR from the probe; unbaked ornament takes both from the probe. Interior ambient 0.4 -> 0.08.
- **3. NO TONE CURVE INSIDE.** (PostFX header, corrected in the exterior pass.) The hall went out as clamped scene-linear: no toe, no shoulder. It now gets FilmGrade's filmic curve with its own print (HALL_GRADE): no green band, warm ivory highlights, neutral shadows. Bloom is per set: inside the 0.82 threshold caught every lit ivory wall (a glow with no source); inside it is now HALL_BLOOM (1.25, only the chandelier, sconces and holograms). Vignette lighter inside.
- **4. THE BAKE WAS NOISY, AND ETC1S MADE IT BLOCKY.** Cycles never denoises bakes; the shipped atlas (512 spp, 1500 s cap) measured 4.7% per-texel grain on a flat wall, and ETC1S quantised it into pink/green 4x4 blocks — the "dirty plaster". Re-baked on the EXISTING UVLightmap layout (rebake_hall_lightmap_v7.py; 160 shell objects, layout verified island-for-island against the shipped atlas), 1024 spp adaptive, indirect clamp 8, then finish_hall_lightmap.py: OIDN (Blender's bundled DLL via ctypes, CPU device, island mask as albedo guide, baked normals as normal guide), push-pull dilation into the whole empty atlas (no dark rims at distance), p99.5 normalisation, dithered 8-bit sRGB, UASTC+RDO. Swapped into the GLB (image 3) byte-for-byte with glb_replace_image.py — no re-export.
- **5. THE MATERIALS THAT MAKE A ROOM EXPENSIVE WERE MISSING.** Walnut panels: their UVs ran -2879..2880, so they were painted flat brown; each panel is now re-projected (u across, v down from its top) and carries one book-matched crown-cut walnut sheet (make_walnut_veneer.py: six leaves, cathedral figure up, lacquer roughness from the pores) — one sheet per field, as boiserie is laid. Floor: one quad with a 2 m repeat; re-cut in the shader into 1.1 m slabs (the room is exactly 18 x 14), each its own piece of stone (offset + quarter turn per slab), 2.5 mm joints that break the reflections.
- **THE STATIONS** (StationDressing.tsx). The projector was a 12-triangle steel box: now a turned brass instrument with a crystal lens (lathe, probe-lit). A beam of light rises from the lens to the plan, breathing with emphasis. The hologram's title card and three callouts were BLANK emissive rectangles — the grey slabs over every plan; the storyboard asks for the project's name: now drawn in the site's Playfair/Inter from the project's own data (name, locality, city, plots, available).
- **THE LENS INSIDE** — the storyboard's "135mm f/2.8". LensFocus now runs in the hall on the subject InteriorStage names (the station or the portrait), blur from 0.8 m behind it. Additive layers blend alpha with MIN and stamp their own depth, so the glowing subject stays sharp over the wall behind it.
- **PORTRAIT BUG.** Its emphasis was read off the last two beats, which since the district field are turn-out and city: the About click was gated off at the portrait and its glow lit behind the camera at the door. Found by id now (beatEmphasis), with a test.
- **UI.** Station numbering in Roman numerals over a gilt hairline.
- **THE DOOR, WITHOUT A STALL.** Profiled (CDP CPU profile, production build): every long frame on the way in was a program's FIRST USE — three reads the info log synchronously, which waits for the compile. Four causes, fixed at source:
  - InteriorStage's own pieces (hit boxes, the portrait's light, the nameplate) sat outside the hall's hidden group, so they DREW ON THE LAWN from the moment the hall armed: 1.4 s of getProgramInfoLog mid-scroll, for programs compiled under the exterior's lights and compiled again at the door. The stage is now shown exactly when the hall is.
  - The hall probe's stand-in re-rendered RoomEnvironment as the hall armed (1.3 s). ONE room cube per renderer (hallProbe.ts, roomCube) is now made under the preloader and shared: stand-in, interior scene.environment, dusk's outside. Its PMREM generator is kept, and primed through fromCubemap once, so the capture's blur and cubemap programs are never first-used at the door (was 190 ms).
  - The exterior FOG was removed by a passive effect, so the swap's first frames drew the hall under the lawn's haze and compiled a fogged variant of every hall program on the spot (5.3 s in one frame). It is now a layout effect and leaves in the swap's own commit.
  - The warm-up itself (warmHallPrograms) now builds one or two programs a frame (4 ms budget; it was 186 ms in one call), then makes three's first call on each as its compile finishes (KHR_parallel_shader_compile only), so the door meets programs that are compiled AND introduced. The probe capture renders one face a frame under the veil (it was the longest frame of the passage). The doorway holds its white until the warm is idle (WorldCanvas, HallReadiness), so a visitor who beats it to the door waits under the white instead of on a frozen frame. hallWarm.test.ts (8 tests) pins what it compiles, on what, within what budget, and when the door may open.
- **THE PASSAGE ITSELF.** (a) The blur's edge fix scales the canvas frame with a CSS transform, and r3f measured the frame's BOUNDING BOX, transforms included: every blur step read as a resize and reallocated the renderer and every composer buffer mid-passage (149 ms in setSize at the swap, the canvas "growing" 1280 -> 1329 px). The Canvas now measures layout size (`resize={{ offsetSize: true }}`). (b) The hall's textures went up to the GPU inside the capture's first face (98 ms); the warm now uploads each material's textures with it (initTexture), in the same budget. The capture is 28-30 ms over six frames; the swap's frames 67-100 ms.
- **THE WHITE HALF-FRAME AT THE SECOND STATION WAS REAL.** An earlier pass logged it as a headless screenshot artifact. Traced pass by pass: ONE NaN pixel in the scene render, from the projector beam's shader (`pow()` of a base a rounding error below zero — |dot| of unit vectors past 1.0 at a grazing pixel, an interpolated v past 1.0 on the rim), gathered by the lens into 252 pixels and spread by the bloom's mip chain into a flat slab at the film curve's ceiling over 60% of the frame, hologram and table gone. It depended on the exact camera pose, so it came and went. Both bases clamped (and the hologram side-glow's, which had the same pattern); the lens pass now drops any non-finite sample, so a stray NaN from any material stays one black pixel. 3/3 runs of the sequence that reproduced it are clean, 0 NaN pixels.
- **TWO LOOK BUGS FOUND ON THE WAY.** (a) CityField's threshold clones of the front wall and entry doors: `MeshStandardMaterial.copy()` resets `defines`, so both lost HALL_PROBE and ESTATE_FOCUS — the front wall's bake was lit a second time by ambient and IBL, beside a room that is not, and the lens read it as sky. Defines restored; the clones now follow the probe's capture. (b) The chandelier crystal's transmission forced a second full render of the scene every frame (establishing shot p90 66.7 ms -> 16.7 ms without it); it is now a clear 0.42 glass lit by the probe.
- **MEASURED.** Production build, 1440x900 @2x, headless Chromium on ANGLE/D3D11, which Chromium puts on the laptop's AMD Radeon INTEGRATED GPU, not the RTX 4070 - a conservative case. NOTE: this machine hitches an EMPTY WebGL page 100-250 ms about once a second (verified with nothing else on the GPU), so frame times below are upper bounds and static-camera long frames are the machine's, not the scene's.
  - Hall arming, mid-scroll: 1.4 s + 1.3 s + 186 ms of main-thread stalls -> the warm builds ~48 programs over 0.7-0.9 s in 17-117 ms frames; nothing is drawn or compiled on the lawn.
  - The door: 2.4 s stall at the start of the pass -> no program compiles at the door (program count 78 -> 76 across the swap); probe capture 143-163 ms spread over six frames; worst swap frames 100-233 ms.
  - Inside, stations and portrait: no new programs at any beat (was 3 at the district field's reveal).
  - Lightmap grain 4.7% -> 0.4% per texel on a flat wall; tests 146 passing, tsc clean, lint clean except pre-existing warnings.

There it ran 26/28 in 22.0 m against the last pass's 27/28 in 13.5 m: the hall pass costs a software renderer far more, and two tests ran out of their 2-minute budget with every step slow (a 500 ms wait took 3-6 s). E2E_GPU=1 (new, opt-in) runs the suite on the GPU; there the address-bar test passes and the door test passes 2 of 3 alone, the third failing on its own polling: it read the white's coverage from outside the page every ~80 ms and one slow frame hid a full-white peak that was painted. The page now records the fullest white it painted (data-doorway-peak) and the test reads that. Full GPU run: **27/28 (3.7 m)**. The one failure is real and left open: "the camera flew over the fountain to the door". A browser trace shows the camera's rush through the portico (passage clock 0.20-0.40) losing 330-850 ms to the GPU itself — the GPU process waits on the D3D11 driver in a raster flush, no JavaScript and no shader compile in it — so the rush shows as ~3 frames on this integrated GPU. ## [2026-09-23 IST] The exterior, round 1: trees, lawn, pool, filtering (essence.md, Stage 1)

- **THE BRIEF.** "Currently it looks like a PS2 game." Researched the Awwwards winners (SOTY, SOTM, Developer) and wrote essence.md: eight principles, the estate's idea, the seven PS2 tells measured on the production build, and a staged plan. This round fixes tells 1-3 and the filtering.
- **TREES (tell 1).** Crowns were 140-160 triangles under flat vector-ellipse leaf atlases. The leaf atlases are now rendered in headless Blender from modelled leaf clusters (tools/blender/render_foliage_v7.py: albedo under a white sky plus a camera-space normal pass, per species, 1024 px, zero edge pixels). Crowns are grown as lobed card clusters with a baked per-vertex occlusion (build_estate_v7.card_tree_mesh; exported as COLOR_0). At runtime (exteriorFoliage.ts): light through the leaves toward the sun, per-tree hue and value, wind, and alpha scaled per mip so crowns do not thin to twigs at distance. The first build was near-black (the card and the vertex occlusion multiplied); FOLIAGE_GAIN restores the leaf value.
- **LAWN (tell 2).** The mowing stripes baked into a 6 m tile aliased into a 12-18 m checkerboard at the film's distances. The texture is turf only now; bands are drawn in world space at 1.6 m, view-dependent (they trade places as the camera orbits) and faded before they alias, over two octaves of macro variation (exteriorLawn.ts).
- **FILTERING.** Every exterior texture was read with anisotropy 1; now the GPU's maximum, capped at 8.
- **POOL (tell 3).** Cyan paint -> a dark reflecting pool (colour 0.32, env 4.2).
- **E2E ON THE 4070 BY DEFAULT.** playwright.experience.config.ts now launches with `--use-angle=d3d11 --enable-gpu --ignore-gpu-blocklist --force_high_performance_gpu` unless `CI` or `E2E_SOFTWARE` is set. Without the last flag Chromium picks the AMD iGPU.
- **MEASURED.** 60 fps at 1440x900 @2x on the RTX 4070 and on the iGPU. Unit tests 150 passing; tsc clean; lint only the pre-existing warning. E2E on the 4070: 27/28 in ~3.5 m. The one failure, "Tier-2 pages are ordinary documents", is GLTFLoader blob-texture errors from aborted navigations; it predates this round and passes alone.
- **OPEN.** The sun sits front-left, so both faces the hero sees are lit and nothing models: rotating it is a change to the approved composition (asked). The compound wall reads as a white band across the middle distance; windows, weathering and sky are still Stage 1 work. Better tree models are Stage 2 and need a decision on downloads, paid generation or an artist.

## [2026-09-23..24 IST] The art-direction critique: hero type and grid, the light, the water, the windows, age, kerbs, and trees from scans

- **THE BRIEF.** Two pasted critiques of the hero: clashing type hierarchy, default-looking body copy, a nav off the grid, ASCII arrows, low contrast over the render, flat light, plastic grass and water, cloned trees and flat-card leaves, rigid hedges, razor lawn edges, a static pool, floating props, a tiled facade with no weathering, black-void windows, a low-poly car, loungers in a line, a washed-out sky and a hard horizon, a pixelated logo and an invisible Enquire. The user approved CC0 tree downloads from Poly Haven.
- **TYPE.** The headline is one size and one leading (1.04, was 0.94 with an extra 0.3em before the italic; "Land, in the" touched "districts"); the italic is an inline emphasis. A gilt hairline steps down to the gloss, now `t-hero-lede` (lede size, 1.62 leading, tracked). "In a hurry? Start here" became a structured "Start here" plate. Every "→" glyph is a drawn SVG arrow (components/site/Arrow.tsx) whose shaft draws out on hover.
- **GRID.** Header and hero share a 12-column `site-grid` (globals.css): logo on the copy's left edge, the nav justified across columns 7-10, Enquire on the frame's right edge; the copy spans columns 1-5. On a phone the header is a plain row (the grid's minimum width had widened the page).
- **CONTRAST.** A scrim behind the hero copy (radial from the left; from the top on a phone) and a soft text shadow; the same scrim on every exterior chapter pane (a pseudo-element of the pane, so it fades with the chapter). Eyebrows and body raised from /45-/55 to /72-/82. Enquire is a solid gilt plate. A forced line break that orphaned "here" in the revolution chapter is now `text-wrap: balance`.
- **THE LIGHT.** The sun moved from front-left [-96,26,62] to front-right DAY_SUN [86,25,50] (WorldCanvas), 14 degrees up: the south front rakes warm (measured median 171) and the west flank falls into shade (42) — it lit both before. The key warmed (#FFE1BA). The painted sky's sun moved with it (make_sky_v7.py), and the light leak moved to the sun's side.
- **CONTACT OCCLUSION** without a depth texture (the composer cannot have one, PostFX.tsx): computed in the lens pass from the view depth already in the frame's alpha — 12 taps over a 2 m disc, normal rebuilt from 3-pixel neighbours (a 1-pixel stencil banded on half-float depth). Exterior only. Glass now leaves the alpha alone (custom blend), or it folded its opacity into the depth.
- **THE NaN GUARD, MADE REAL.** ANGLE's D3D compiler warned X3577 that it may fold `isnan()` away — the lens guard from the last round could have been compiled out. It tests the exponent bits now.
- **WATER.** Four wave trains (2.3 m to 0.27 m) in the normal, each faded before it aliases; in the pool, depth from the distance to its edge (lighter shallows, a dark green-black body) and caustics in the shallows.
- **WINDOWS** (exteriorWindows.ts). Interior mapping: each pane's plane is followed into a virtual room — back wall, side walls, walnut floor, ceiling, a lamp, pictures, drapes at the edges, some rooms dark — lit warm, stronger with the evening. Three bugs found on the way, all measured: a world room grid crossed the panes (so each window is found at load as a connected piece and its room centred on it); the curtain's pleats sent rays into sideways rooms (the facade normal is the pane's thin axis now); and a sine hash of an interpolated centre made a different room per pixel (rounded first). Glass is clear (0.26) over them.
- **AGE** (exteriorSurfaces.ts), in world space so it cannot tile: rising damp and splash-back, rain streaks and broad warm/cool variation on the stone; slate-to-slate variation on the roof; leaf-scale colour and normal noise on the hedges.
- **TREES FROM SCANS** (tools/blender/ph_trees_v7.py). Poly Haven CC0: tree_small_02 (rain tree), jacaranda_tree (mango), island_tree_02 (neem) — 362 MB of glTF in C:/dev/Blender/_polyhaven, not in the repo. Each becomes ~3-5k triangles: leaf cards rendered from the scan's own densest outer cluster (feathered by whole leaves, no long limbs), a card crown laid on the scan's actual leaf volume (favouring its surface), and the scan's trunk and larger limbs decimated on their own budgets with its bark maps. build_estate_v7 uses them when the library exists and falls back to the procedural trees; the ship script builds them. Runtime: cards seen edge-on fade out. GLB 9.2 -> 12.1 MB.
- **BUILD.** Stone kerbs on every lawn-facing walk edge; the five loungers yawed and spaced as people leave them, backrests at different rakes. The car paint is a clear-coated physical material.
- **MEASURED.** Production build, 1440x900 @2x: 60 fps on the RTX 4070 and on the AMD iGPU. Unit tests 154 (new: exteriorWindows.test.ts). E2E on the 4070: **28/28 (3.0 m)**. The mobile test now also asserts the layout is the device's width — it had passed while a scrim widened the phone layout to 484px.

## [2026-09-24 IST] The agent's review against Vertex3D: the ball, the camera, the hall, the threshold, the footer, the land

- **THE BRIEF.** A Quality Homes agent sent a screen capture of Vertex3D's site (frames pulled with Blender's video decoder; no ffmpeg on this machine) and two critiques: cloned palm arrays, billboard trees, a flat plane of terrain, a raw particle sphere, a robotic camera, a camera through closed doors, a washed-out hall with no occlusion, low-resolution carpet, a flat portrait, grey CAD models on the tables, text over bright 3D, and a footer that reads as a separate page.
- **ALREADY TRUE OF THIS BUILD** (the critique was made of an older one): the passage opens the doors and flies into their light (frames captured through a wheel-driven passage), the trees are 3D scan-derived crowns, and the post-processing has bloom.
- **THE BALL** (Constellation.tsx), with the Vertex3D sphere as the reference the storyboard was waiting for: ~1,300 instanced gilt beads drawn out along their radii, hot at the face and amber at the rim, bright enough to bloom, with an additive aura that leaves the depth alpha alone. The hover drags beads along the pointer's smoothed velocity across the ball and stretches them as they go, so a sweep pulls a streaming tail that relaxes back over ~1.5 s.
- **THE CAMERA** (cameraSpring.ts). The first-order lag became a critically damped spring at the same time constant: the same trailing distance, but it accelerates out of rest and decelerates into a stop, and settles sooner. A few centimetres of handheld drift outside passages (off under reduced motion). Tests pin the start from rest, no overshoot and frame-rate independence.
- **THE HALL.** The print down a third of a stop with more contrast (HALL_GRADE exposure 2.3 -> 1.95, contrast 0.1 -> 0.2; mean luma 112 -> 89 at the establishing shot) and a heavier vignette; contact occlusion inside at 0.7 m for the unbaked pieces (tables, urns, balusters, nosings). The runner has tufts, pile and a velvet sheen in world space (hallDetail.ts); the portrait's glass reflects the room through the probe and keeps the depth alpha; the painting has a varnish sheen. The holograms have a travelling scan line and a faint raster.
- **TEXT.** The hall's copy panes have the chapter scrim (denser inside) and raised contrast.
- **THE THRESHOLD.** At the district field one wall jamb (the walnut panelling beside the door) hung down the left of the frame: the camera stands half a metre further into the doorway (city beat z 5.7 -> 6.2) and the aim offset fades out on the way, so both jambs are outside the frame.
- **THE FOOTER.** On the film it is the last frame: transparent at its head over the district field, deepening to the page colour, gilt hairlines, and a sign-off in the film's type ("The land is best seen from the land." / Arrange a site visit). Other pages keep the plain footer.
- **THE LAND** (build_estate_v7.py). The park rolls: long, incommensurate swells up to ~1 m, level on the formal core, the avenue corridor, the helipad and a margin inside the wall. Four royal palm variants at different heights and leans, each avenue palm a random variant, jittered along and across the line.
- **MEASURED.** 60 fps on the RTX 4070 and on the AMD iGPU (including at the ball). Unit tests 157; the GLSL reserved-word test now covers the new shader files. E2E on the 4070: **28/28 (3.1 m)**.
- Backups: C:/dev/Blender/mansion_estate_V7.before_land.blend, exterior_estate_v7.before_land.glb. Nothing committed.

## [2026-09-24 IST] Approved: the imperial hall, a photographed sky, and a ball with a body

- **THE BRIEF.** The client approved the two held items — "a double-curved imperial staircase, triple the ceiling height, and a portrait at a central landing", and a photographic sky from Poly Haven — and found the ball "still rigid", its hover "cheap and rigid": "make the particles a little rigid to stay together but the ball itself and the hover effects must be effortless".
- **THE BALL HAS A BODY** (ballSim.ts). Every bead has mass and is held by springs — to its place, and to its six nearest neighbours' displacement, so a disturbance travels across the surface as a patch rather than moving one bead (the "a little rigid"). The ball as a whole floats on a soft spring and has a squash mode along the direction it was pushed; the pointer is a wide force field that drags beads along its motion and parts them round it, applied as force, not position, so the response builds, carries on, overshoots a little and settles by itself over about a second. A slow flow field and three breathing waves keep it from ever being still. Everything lightly underdamped (damping ratios 0.4-0.5), substepped at 90 Hz and identical at 30/60/144 fps. The simulation's own arrays are the instance attributes (no copy per frame); a bead riding the ball's sway is not brightened by it — only its own motion stirs it. Tuned on captured sequences: the first pass swayed the ball but barely moved the surface under the hand; the second (softer home springs, stiffer coupling, a wider, stronger field) combs a visible current through the beads that settles back. Tests: ballSim.test.ts (5).
- **THE SKY IS PHOTOGRAPHED** (tools/gltf/make_sky_ph_v7.py). Poly Haven's kloppenheim_06_puresky (CC0; 4K .hdr, 17.4 MB, in C:/dev/Blender/_polyhaven/hdri, not in the repo) — a "pure sky" with its land removed, because the last photographic HDRI's horizon of hills and a village is what the client called "a slum". Turned about the vertical so its sun sits on the key light's bearing (the key keeps its approved 14 degrees; the photographed sun stands at 4.6); exposed to the painted sky's median plus a third of a stop, with a film shoulder so the lit cumulus roll off rather than clip; the painted sky's haze put back below the horizon; and in the lighting copy the photographed sun disc is taken down to its glow, so no polished surface reflects a second sun. The fog colour already sat between the new horizon's warm and cool sides. The painted sky is kept (C:/dev/Blender/_sky_procedural_v7, and make_sky_v7.py).
- **THE IMPERIAL HALL** (tools/blender/imperial_hall_v7.py, on the extended hall).
  - *The stair*: a horseshoe — two curved flights 2 m wide, springing apart from the floor and climbing 119 degrees round a central court to a landing 4.2 m up against the back wall; 26 risers of 16 cm, 30 cm goings on the walking line; solid stone with a raked soffit. One balustrade runs unbroken up the right flight, across the landing's curved front and down the left; the outer rails die into the back wall; newels at the four feet and the flights' heads (the site turns them and the 127 balusters, hallJoinery.ts). A runner on each flight and across the landing, held by brass rods; a walnut panel on the court's face of the landing.
  - *The height*: walls 8 -> 13 m. The old cornice is kept as the string course; above it an attic order (the room's own pilasters at 0.59), arched clerestory windows between them (six a side, three over the entry, two on the back wall), and a second cornice. The coffered ceiling opens into a ribbed dome 10.8 m across with its oculus at 19.2 m — three times the 6.4 m of the room as first built. Three times the extended hall's 8 m would have put the ceiling twice as high as the house's own parapet; the dome under the lantern is how a house of this type is tall inside.
  - *The portrait*: a quarter larger, over the central landing, on an arched walnut panel in a cream architrave that breaks through the string course (the frieze and cornice open round it). The chandelier hangs under the dome on a chain from the oculus ring, 45% larger. The urns flank the entry doors (the flights reach where they stood).
  - *The bake*: a new room, so a new lightmap layout (bake_lightmap.py: 277 shell objects, 62,691 triangles; the concave UV pack alone is ~20 min of CPU), then the shipped atlas re-baked on it at 768 spp, OIDN-finished and UASTC-encoded (rebake_hall_lightmap_v7.py, finish_hall_lightmap.py). The divisor is 8.294 (was 3.857): the oculus's pool of light under the dome and the windows' wash on the attic walls now set the atlas's 99.5th percentile. The oculus is a disc light and the windows are area lights behind emissive panes, so the room's light has visible sources. GLB 24.4 MB (was 23.4). A first pass with the chandelier at 8.5 m put it on the portrait beat's axis; it was raised 1.3 m and the room re-baked before shipping.
  - *The site*: the portrait's hit box, hover glow, picture light and light fan follow the frame; the nameplate's walnut mount is now the GLB's arched panel (lit by the bake), so the site builds only the brass plate; the room's reflection box rises to 13 m; the walnut veneer dresses the new panels, one veneer per source material so the baked ones keep their bake.
- **THE FILM, RE-CUT** (interiorPath.ts). The threshold and the wide shot aim up (the height is the room's point); the crossing looks up at the horseshoe as it passes its feet; S2 is approached down a lane outside the left flight and seen from the front (the old viewpoint is on the treads now); the climb is a rise through the court between the flights — a new beat — to a portrait beat 8.2 m up, which the landing's rail sets: from lower, it crossed the engraved plate. The collision test models the flights as wedges built from IMPERIAL_STAIR, the numbers the Blender script used, instead of a hand-drawn hull; portraitNameplate.test.ts holds the plate's sight line over the rail.
- **MEASURED.** 60 fps (p95 16.8 ms) at every hall beat on the RTX 4070 and on the AMD iGPU. Unit tests 182 (new: ballSim.test.ts; portraitNameplate.test.ts rewritten, including the plate's sight line over the rail; the interior collision test now models the curved flights). Lint 0. E2E on the 4070: **28/28 (3.2 m)**. Captures: the establishing shot holds both flights, the attic windows, the coffers and the dome's rim; the portrait beat holds the frame, its arch and the plate.
- Backups: C:/dev/Blender/_hall_before_imperial/ (mansion_web_V7HALL.blend, interior_hall.glb, its manifest); C:/dev/Blender/_sky_procedural_v7/. Nothing committed.

## [2026-09-30 IST] The station tables' bases, and station 3 re-shot

- **THE BRIEF.** "The bases of these 3 stations must be fixed; the camera angle of station 3 ... change it fully." Screenshots: a near-black pedestal standing on a flat pale disc.
- **THE BASE WAS INSIDE OUT.** Measured on the source mesh (table_base, shared by all four tables): its side walls face inward (normal . radial -1.00), its sloping tops face down and its underside faces UP, lying at exactly the floor's height. Rendered single-sided, the camera saw through the near wall to the inside of the far one (the black lump) and through the foot to that underside disc on the floor (the pale disc). The table tops were partly inverted too, which hid half their inlay ring.
- **AND IT WAS BAKED WRONG.** The four tables share one mesh per part and were lightmapped anyway — four tables baked into the same texels — and they turn when dragged, which no bake can follow. The underside disc also acted as a lid in the bake: the floor atlas was pure black under every foot, and mip filtering spread that black past the rim; the station's ADDITIVE floor glow then tinted it tan.
- **THE FIX.** HallModel.liveTurntables: every mesh under a TURNTABLE_* node takes a live material (no lightmap, so the probe lights it) drawn double-sided — three turns a back face's normal toward the viewer, so the inside-out walls shade as outer surfaces — and the floor-level underside is removed. tools/gltf/clear_table_shadows.py filled the four footprints in the floor atlas from the light around them (re-encoded UASTC, swapped into image 3). The station glow now MULTIPLIES what is beneath it instead of adding to it: a soft shadow under the top, a contact line at the foot and a faint warm lift that follows the emphasis — light scales the marble's own colour and cannot paint a dark patch tan. Tests: hallTables.test.ts (2).
- **STATION 3.** Every plan is raked 38 degrees up and turned square to its own wall (measured: S3's faces -x). The old viewpoint ([-0.62, -0.78], chosen to dodge the urns, which are at the doors now) stood 51 degrees off that, so the plan read as a skewed sliver jammed into the corner. It is now shot the way S1 is, mirrored — from the room onto the right wall, [-0.94, 0.34], 20 degrees off square (dead square stands on the right flight's balustrade; this is 0.8 m outside it), 3.3 m out on a 30-degree lens, down a straight lane at x 5.0. It is also the storyboard's A3 shot: the camera faces right, at A3.
- **MEASURED.** Unit tests 184, E2E on the 4070 **28/28 (3.3 m)**. Captures of all three stations and their approaches. Nothing committed.

## [2026-09-30 IST] The art-direction audit: an editorial type system, a quiet cover, a bound sphere, a milled model, window light, a lens

- **THE BRIEF.** A new art-direction audit ("the UI of a B2B SaaS dashboard over a 3D world ... confusing effects with sophistication"). The client: do everything in it except removing the door transition, which stays as designed.
- **TYPE AND UI** (globals.css, page, SiteHeader, ConsentPanel). The scale comes down (display max 4.3 -> 3.5rem; body 15-17 -> 14-15.5px), the grotesque reads at 300 and labels at 400, capitals open from 0.22em to 0.32em, and a micro label and a serif figure style join the scale. Actions are hairlines, not plates: the primary a faint ivory rule round the label that turns gilt on hover; Enquire a gilt hairline over a light veil instead of a solid bronze pill. The sphere's figures are set as an editorial table — Playfair figures under micro labels, hairline rules between the columns, a wide gap before the gloss. The consent dialog is on the same system (its teal focus ring was the one colour outside the palette).
- **THE COVER** (Preloader). No 8rem counter: the house's name in micro capitals over a hairline that fills, dissolving over 1.4 s. On a return within the session, when the scene is already in memory, the cover starts clear and is drawn only if loading is still going after 450 ms — no black screen on coming back from a project page.
- **THE SPHERE** (Constellation, cameraPath). Twice the points, each a fine round dot on the exact lattice, frost at the face and champagne at the limb, just over the bloom threshold; held to the surface (radial offset clamped to 2.5% of the radius) so the hover sweeps points ACROSS the sphere and the silhouette stays true. Radius 9.92 -> 4.6 and centre 33.3 -> 26.4: 17.7% of the held frame's height (the test now holds it between 14% and 21%), still crowning the spire. A champagne point light at its centre rises with the chapter and casts on the roof and lawn; always mounted, so the exterior's programs never recompile.
- **THE DISTRICT FIELD** (CityField). The navy plane with drawn contours and glowing orbs is a physical model: relief displaced from the same noise, satin champagne-silver with milled grooves and engraved contours, one warm raking key pooled on the centre, dark at every edge; the markers are brass pins (a shaded head on a needle) standing on the relief, their bases read from a TypeScript port of the shader's noise.
- **THE HALL.** Light through the clerestory (WindowLight.tsx): one shaft per sun-side window, a sheared prism mapped to a unit box so the view ray is slab-tested and integrated exactly — a true volume in one pass, crisp-edged, thinning along its length, with drifting density and dust; sized to end where the sill's edge meets the floor. The print a quarter-stop down with more contrast (exposure 1.95 -> 1.72, contrast 0.2 -> 0.27). The plans nearly monochrome (chroma 0.9 -> 0.18, plot faces 0.55 -> 0.25) on a frosted-glass pane with an etched edge — the "gold coin" beside the Kartikeya plan was its north-point stamp printed in yellow, and is a drawn compass now.
- **THE LENS** (LensFocus, PostFX). Camera motion blur in the lens pass, from the depth already in the alpha: each pixel re-projected with the last frame's camera, gathered along half its screen travel (a 180-degree shutter), nothing under 1.5 px, capped at 28 px, zero on a camera cut and under reduced motion. Chromatic aberration 0.00042 -> 0.0009 at the corners only.
- **MEASURED.** Unit tests 187, typecheck and lint clean, E2E on the 4070 **28/28 (3.4 m)**. 60 fps (p95 16.8 ms) on the RTX 4070 and the AMD iGPU in the hero, at the sphere with the pointer sweeping it, and at every hall beat. Captures of the whole film, a fast pan mid-move and settled, and the cover. Nothing committed.
