## [2026-09-30 IST] Issue: third art-direction critique (the user chose: blur behind the copy, a quieter frame, a weathered facade)
- Found: the critique was written from the 07:25 recording, so its boxed "Start here", black footer block, scrims and sphere were already gone, and its camera point (inertia, easing, idle drift) was already in place. What still held in the current build, measured on clean plates (the frame with the DOM hidden) at 1440x900: the hero headline over the golden-hour cloud bank (mean luma 141, p90 180; the italic's band to 197); the holdings heading over the sunset for the first half of its chapter (160..203); its gloss over a sunlit lawn at dusk (p90 186); the hall's opening line over the clerestory windows (p90 243); four nav links, two hero actions, a scroll cue and a sound control on the cover; a facade of one flat beige; backlit leaf cards at dusk each glowing whole and split red/cyan by the aberration. Its "physical models on the tables" point was not built: the user has decided the hologram stations stay.
- Fix applied:
  - THE LENS SWINGS OFF THE COPY (copyLens.ts, ChapterFade, LensFocus). ChapterFade already reads each chapter's content rect per scroll frame; it now also publishes the visible copy's text box (a Range, so the text's own extent) and how much of it is on screen. The lens swings its plane of focus away from that side of the frame, as a tilt-shift architecture lens does: the copy's side goes soft (two Kawase fields, half and quarter resolution, rendered only while copy is up), full to just past the words and back to sharp over a fifth of the frame; the house's projected box holds 70% of its focus over a wide feather (cut out whole, it drew a seam). The focus pulls in 0.45 s as words arrive and leave. The station and portrait chapters opt out (data-copy-lens-off): their long lens is on the subject, and those frames were approved. Copy wider than 55% of the frame (a phone's column) does not swing it.
  - COPY ON THE LAND (page, from md up). Measured bands decided it: the hero block stands at the foot of the frame, on the soft lawn, with the sky left to the spire (headline mean 141 -> 72); the revolution's lines sit 8vh lower (eyebrow p90 177 -> 105); the holdings block at the foot of its frame (heading 160..203 -> 46..82 across the chapter); the hall's opening line under the sconces, clear of the windows. A phone keeps its own layout (its foot is the facade); its hero block is lifted 4vh so the italic clears the cloud bank.
  - A CLOUD FOR THE EVENING (softSunShadows). The holdings copy looks down on the lawn north-east of the house; raycast through the copy block at every beat from 0.40 to 0.625, the ground stays inside one patch of sun space, so one cloud covers the chapter, kept off every ray that reaches the house (HOUSE_RAYS) so the house keeps its evening light. The gloss's ground: mean 79..97 -> 28..37.
  - A QUIETER FRAME. Over the film the header is the mark, Enquire and one "Menu" (masthead capitals, two hairlines that close to a cross); the panel is the navigation at every width there, set as a contents page on desktop, holds Lenis while open (a hidden overflow does not stop it; measured: a 1200 px wheel over the open menu moved the page 0 px) and lets its own list scroll (data-lenis-prevent). Other pages keep their bar. The hero keeps one action ("See the layouts raised" is "Enter the hall" in the menu). The scroll cue appears only after 3.2 s untouched on the cover and leaves at the first scroll (ScrollCue); the sound control arrives half a viewport in (or lit from the start for a visitor who turned it on before).
  - THE MENU HAD NO GROUND. `bg-[#060A14]/97` is not a step of Tailwind 3's opacity scale and generated no CSS, so the phone menu had always shown the page through its links; now `/[0.97]`. Seven more classes are silently missing the same way — the inner pages' header band `bg-[#0A1120]/72`, and text at /42 /62 /72 /76 /82 /88 (every chapter eyebrow, the hero lede, the colophon's addresses), which have always rendered at full strength. Left as they are and flagged: fixing them changes the look the reviews have seen.
  - WEATHER (exteriorSurfaces, exteriorFoliage, PostFX). Stone: stronger patina (+-10%/7%, warm/cool), grime from the baked occlusion sampled into the colour (the shelter of cornices, sills, reveals), dribbles down the walls under the string course (4.80 m) and the entablature (8.50 m) — not the trim, where they made a row of dashes along the portico's beam — a ragged greener damp line at the foot, and rougher stone wherever it is dirty. Foliage: the evening takes 70% of the light through the leaves, so a backlit crown reads as a mass with a rim. Chromatic aberration 0.0009 -> 0.00065.
  - Also: the resume label for the ending reads "the land" (was "the map room"); stale comments naming the removed sphere and the map room updated.
- Verified: tsc, eslint, vitest 179/179; Playwright experience 28/28 on the 4070 (3.1 min, twice: before and after the last fixes); 60 fps (p95 16.7..16.8 ms) on the RTX 4070 and the AMD iGPU at the hero and the holdings (copy up, both soft fields rendering), the hall's wide shot, S1, S3 and the land (one iGPU run hitched to p95 50 ms at the holdings; two repeats measured 16.8). Menu checked on desktop and phone: focus enters, Escape returns it, the page does not scroll underneath, scrolling resumes on close. Captures in scratchpad audit2/c3/.
- Flagged for review: not committed. The seven ungenerated opacity classes above.
- THEN, THE SAME DAY: the user rejected the blur ("remove the blur it is shit u made it so wrong"). The swing is removed entirely: copyLens.ts deleted, LensFocus back to its depth of field, contact occlusion and motion blur only, ChapterFade back to the fade alone, the station and portrait opt-outs gone. The depth of field from the Vertex3D pass is untouched. The copy positions stay: measured again on clean plates WITHOUT the blur, the new places still read far darker than the old ones (hero headline mean luma 70 vs 139, revolution 41 vs 64, holdings at 0.40 58 vs 128, the hall's wide shot 66 vs 145), though with more detail behind them (the hero's gloss now crosses the pool terrace). Verified after the removal: tsc, eslint, vitest 179/179, E2E on the 4070 28/28 (3.1 min).

## [2026-10-01 IST] Issue: fourth art-direction critique (everything in it except the stations, which stay as they are)
- Found: the critique ("a Unity engine tech-demo ... the 3D environment and the 2D UI are treating each other as enemies") was written from an older recording, so some of what it names was already gone. What still held, measured on the current build: the mark on an ivory plate with a ring and a shadow; a panel of white light behind the door and a white DOM layer at the swap; a studio portrait at the screen's own light, its top taking 97 times the light of its middle; a lawn whose 6 m tile recurs in a lattice at 40 m; lit rooms at dusk that threw no light on the ground; a last chapter outside the house (a contoured field with brass obelisks under a night sky); three faces, not two — Playfair Display, Inter, and the operating system's Georgia and Segoe UI wherever a page inherited body or used `font-serif`; italics inside headlines; sizes off any scale; and, once the header's bar and the hero's arrow were taken away, copy and controls standing on bright picture (hero header over a p90 luma of 137 to 181, the holdings heading over 209, five inner pages at 1.1 to 1.9:1). Its "maquettes on the tables" point was NOT built: the user excluded the stations.
- Fix applied:
  - NO SHADOW ON ANY TYPE, AND LEGIBILITY MADE IN THE LENS (lensFilter.ts, FilmGrade, copyZone.ts, ChapterFade). The critique's three means, in its order. *Dim the light behind the text*: the hall's house lights follow the copy (hallLight.houseKeys: down while the ivory room's copy is up, held at each table for as long as its copy stands), and every reading page lowers the world to a reading light (readingLight.ts; /start-here holds the film's first frame at the film's own night instead of running the film in 891 px). *A photographic ND filter*: a graduated neutral density in scene-linear light BEFORE the print's curve, so the sky comes down into the shoulder rather than under a grey — a soft ellipse for the holdings' sunset, a band across the top of the frame for the header (2.6 stops by day, letting go as the evening falls; 2.2 in the hall with the band's highlights burnt in under a ceiling, because a window behind the header is a light source no density brings down), an off-frame hard-edged ellipse that darkens the copy's edge of the frame at the tables, a foot grad for the approach, and on a phone a grad that RIDES with the copy as ChapterFade publishes where it stands. The filter arrives before the copy and leaves after it (copyPresence). *Position*: chapter copy placed on the picture's quiet ground by measurement, per breakpoint. A chapter now dissolves from the moment it leaves its sticky line, and no line passes through the header.
  - THE MARK, REVERSED OUT (Logo.tsx, qhr-mark-reversed.svg). No plate, ring or shadow: the house and its H in ivory, the Q in gilt, the same paths as the flat mark, straight on the picture. One colour alone merges the H's stem into the Q. Enquire and Menu are reversed out the same way over the film.
  - THE DOOR IS DARK (doorway.ts, DoorwayRig, passageLight.ts). The leaves open on an unlit vestibule with a far glimmer; the camera accelerates into it with the lens holding the door frame; the models swap in that dark under a near-black layer that covers only frames already black; inside, the exposure starts five stops under and the iris opens as the camera decelerates — windows and chandelier first, then the walls — while the focus racks from the door to the far wall. Nothing in the passage lifts the exposure above rest (doorway.test).
  - THE PORTRAIT IS IN THE ROOM (hallDetail.ts, WorldCanvas PORTRAIT_LIGHT). The print graded as a print hung there: dyes quieter and warmed to the lamps, whites held under paper, falling off toward its edges, a shade brighter on the clerestory's side, the room's haze in front of it. The glass reflects what faces it — the arched window over the entry and the two beside it, glazing bars and all, by a reflected ray, so the window slides across the pane as the camera climbs. Lit by a framing spot 3.2 m out instead of a point 28 cm from the canvas.
  - THE HERO (exteriorLawn, LensFocus, page). The lawn read twice (as laid, and at 0.61 scale turned 37 degrees), mixed by a 9 m noise with a 4 m clump field: no lattice. Contact occlusion deeper (1.0 -> 1.25). The arrow is gone (Arrow.tsx deleted): "Start here" is a hairline and a label.
  - DUSK AND NIGHT (nightPools.ts, ExteriorModel, exteriorFoliage). Every lit window throws the shape of its opening onto the ground in front of it — a trapezoid from the sill, bright near the wall — baked once into a 12 cm map over the estate's plan that every ground material reads; the pool reflects a second probe taken at night; frangipani petals no longer print as lamps (PETAL_GAIN). Blacks left deep.
  - THE DESERT IS A TABLE (MapTable.tsx, mapTablePlan.ts, tools/gltf/make_map_table.py, interiorPath). CityField, Terrain, cityLayout and the night-sky coda are deleted. The film ends inside: a round walnut map table in the court of the stair, the two districts in plaster relief in a brass bezel (lacquered sea, gilt rivers, the ghats), lit by the clerestory's low sun with the ranges' shadows ray-marched offline; a brass pin per published layout, each a real link. The coda is the room's own: the house lights go down round the table.
  - THE MENU ON THE GRID (SiteHeader, globals.css). Columns on the site's grid, every size a step of the scale; the page's copy is hidden while the panel is open (it ghosted through the 97% ground).
  - ONE FAMILY, ONE SCALE, ONE BASELINE (layout.tsx, globals.css, tailwind.config, every page). Playfair — the 2.0 of Playfair Display, same designer and licence, with an optical-size axis so one face carries a 10 px label and a 61 px figure. A fluid modular scale (1.2 at 360 px, 1.25 at 1440 px), nine steps, the only sizes in use; line heights whole multiples of 4 px; no italic anywhere (emphasis by weight). Seven off-scale Tailwind opacity classes that had never generated CSS now exist (`/[0.62]`, `/[0.68]`...), and the enquiry form is restyled for the dark site.
  - THE PHONE'S FRAME (phoneFraming.ts). Below a 3:4 aspect the non-station interior beats take a wider lens and a shifted front (setViewOffset), so the stair, the portrait and the map table are composed for a column; the lens pass rebuilds positions with the frustum's off-centre terms, without which a shifted frame smeared as motion.
  - ONE QUESTION FOR THE PAGE AND THE LENS (copyZone.filmIsWide, Tailwind `wide:`, ChapterFade, lensFilter.tableBandFilter). Found in this pass's own checks, on screens the film had never been captured on. The page chose its layout by width (md) and the lens its filters by aspect, so a tablet held upright got a landscape frame's copy under a phone's sky grad, which followed the words down the frame and took the daylight hero with it, house and all. Both now ask one predicate: wide is a landscape frame from 768 px up. On every other frame the tables' copy no longer lies across the plan under an ellipse of density (measured lens on against lens clear at 390x844: it took the plan itself down two stops): it stands in four lines under the header, above the plan, the header's own band comes down over it and is gone before the pane begins, and it develops in place once the camera is on the table. The map's list develops in place too (it used to climb over the table's lit relief: its note at 76% on a p90 luma of 211). The cover on a phone is set for the frame a phone actually shows with its browser's bars up (390x664, not the 390x844 of its glass): the headline a step down, the block ending on the roof's slate at 46% of the frame, where it had run to 60% and taken the grad over the house. A pane at nothing takes no pointer (an unseen link was clickable on its way through the frame), and a keyboard that focuses a link in a chapter off screen is taken to where that chapter is held (the focus used to land on a name at opacity 0).
  - THE OCCLUSION JUDGES PAIRS (LensFocus contactAO). Found in this pass's own close inspection: fine diagonal hatching over the front in the approach. The depth is a half float, so the normal rebuilt from it is wrong by up to forty degrees, and every flat wall occluded itself in a contour map of the depth's steps. The twelve taps are now six pairs through the pixel and the SUM of a pair's elevations is what is counted: on a flat surface a tilted normal lifts one tap as far as it sinks the other. A receiver-plane shadow bias tried first for the same hatch did nothing for it and stippled small shadows; it was taken out again.
  - THE FILM'S STAGE: ONE COMPOSITION AT EVERY SIZE OF WIDE FRAME (globals.css `.film-stage`, tailwind.config, site-home, SiteHeader, SiteFooter's colophon, lensFilter STAGE). Found by this pass's own checks, on the screens the film is actually seen on. Every measurement the copy was placed by was taken at 1440x900 — and no browser window is that: this machine's is 1536x730, its monitor's 1920x945. The picture scales with the frame's HEIGHT (a camera keeps its vertical field of view) and the copy was set in rems on a 72rem grid, so on any other frame the two came apart: at 1536x730 the cover began 40% of the way down instead of 50% with its place line on the horizon's haze (p90 luma 110) and the holdings' eyebrow stood in the sunset (157 to 181); at 1920x1080 the grid stood 110px further in than the house it was set against and the tables' burnt edge sat on the frame's edge while the copy stood a fifth of the way in. Now, on a wide frame, the film's copy is set in the frame's own unit: `--u`, one pixel of the 1440x900 design — a nine-hundredth of the frame's height, or a twelve-hundredth of its width where it is squarer than 4:3. The type scale, the baseline, every space (Tailwind's spacing scale is said in baselines: `calc(var(--bl) * n)`, the same rems everywhere else), the grid's width and the header's height are counts of it, the grid stays centred, and the lens places its filters by the frame's aspect the same way. The same block stands over the same part of the picture, the same size against it, from 1024x768 to 3440x1300 (measured: the copy's left edge 552 units left of the frame's middle at every one of them). Type never goes below a scale of its own that can be read (ratio 1.18 on 14px, 10px capitals), in rems; where that makes a block taller than it was drawn, it is held by its TOP, at the design's height in the picture, and grows downward into the foot of the frame.
  - A PHONE ON ITS SIDE (copyZone SHORT_QUERY, Tailwind `short:`). A landscape frame no taller than 520px is a wide frame whatever its width — the other layout stacks copy across the top of a frame that has no top to stack it in (the cover's block alone is 260px of a 360px frame) — and a short one: its headings follow the frame down to floors of their own (a 21px headline, an 18px name), each chapter keeps its statement and takes its label and gloss off the picture (`short:sr-only`: still in the page for a screen reader, never display:none on copy), the establishing copy keeps its first sentence, no headline carries gilt (it is small text there), and the holdings' grad opens as dense as the upright phone's.
  - EVERY LINE AT AA AT ITS OWN STRENGTH (site-home, StationText, CityLink, the colophon, ScrollCue). The grounds had been measured for ivory at full strength; the small lines were set at 42 to 75% of it. Measured per text node, with its real colour and opacity, against the clean plate under it: the cover's place line at 4.1:1, the figures' labels 3.2 to 3.9, a table's place line 3.8 and its count in gilt 3.5, the colophon's links and offices 4.0 to 4.4. Small capitals and statements are ivory at full strength now, a gloss at 88 to 95%; gilt only where it is large or its ground is dark (a table's count is ivory, its numeral gilt); the figures run Open, Plots, Layouts so the gilt one stands at the row's left end, where the land stays dark all chapter (p90 28 to 70 against 27 to 175 at the right); the holdings' grad reaches down over the figures and opens 0.4 stop denser; the cover wears half a stop under its copy.
  - THE SOUND CONTROL IS IN THE HEADER (AmbientSound, a portal into a slot SiteHeader keeps; globals.css). It stood in the bottom-right corner of the picture, on whatever the film put there: at the first and third tables the pale wall beside the table, ivory capitals on a p90 luma of 136 to 145, 2.1 to 2.3:1. The one edge the lens holds dark for type is the top, so it is set there, beside Enquire and Menu, in their vocabulary (bars alone on a phone).
  - THE LAST TABLE'S COPY LEAVES WITH THE CAMERA (ChapterFade OUT_ATTR). The camera withdraws from the third table to the stair and the hall's bare wall comes round behind the copy's column; leaving over the standard quarter of a viewport the lines were still at half strength on it (p90 99 to 114 at leg 0.57, across four sizes of frame). That one pane leaves over 0.12.
  - THE INNER PAGES, STRICT (Surface, SiteHeader, SiteFooter, hall, start-here, readingLight READING_CEILING, FilmGrade). Checked top to bottom at every scroll position with each line's real colour: the footer's "Head office"/"Branch" in bronze at 70% (3.6:1) are the menu's gilt at the menu's strength; the header's bar is 80% (at 72% a white picture passing under it left the links at 4.4:1); a surface's ground is 86% at its lightest, so its quietest small text reads at AA over anything the world can put behind it (a daylit facade behind the testimonials left their captions at 4.0 to 4.4:1 — and every line of a page scrolls through the top of the frame); the hall's caption is 80 to 100%; and Start here, whose copy stands on the lit house, holds that house under a ceiling in the print (nothing above a luma of 77: a lit window is a light source, and at one stop under it still printed at 111 behind a place line, 2.7:1) with nothing on the page under 75%.
  - THE HEADER BAND HAD A LOWER EDGE (FilmGrade `shoulder`). Found on this pass's own frames: a faint horizontal line across the hall's white wall, and across its windows, at 24% of the frame's height. The band's ceiling was eased in with the band, but the shoulder's knee was always seven-tenths of the ceiling, so the band's first sliver already rolled off everything above a luma of 178 by up to a ninth (a step of 6 to 16 between two rows). The knee now comes down off the ceiling over the first third of the band: at nothing, nothing moves. The same shoulder holds a reading page's highlights.
  - THE COLOPHON DOES NOT PASS THROUGH THE HEADER (ChapterFade `lines` mode, SiteFooter). Over the film the header has no bar, and on any frame shorter than the colophon its lines rode up through the mark and the controls on the way to the page's end (twelve of them under the header at 390x844). Each line now dissolves at the header's edge, as a chapter's does.
  - A SQUARE FRAME IS A PHONE'S, TO THE LENS TOO (copyZone.filmIsWide). CSS calls a square `portrait`; the lens called it wide. One predicate again, and a test holds it.
  - THE FILM'S LAST FRAME IS COMPOSED FOR THE COLOPHON THAT STANDS ON IT (codaFrame.ts, phoneFraming PHONE_CODA, lensFilter.codaFilter, FilmGrade ndAll, ChapterFade's lines, MapTable). Found by this pass's own check of the page's end, line by line: the coda had been written as a move (the house lights go down round the map table, the camera rises off it) and its last frame fell where the move ended — the lit table in the middle of the frame, a third of the way up from its foot, which is where the colophon's links, offices and legal line stand. At 1440x900 `About` stood on the table's rim (p90 luma 115, 3.4:1); at 1536x730 `Gallery` on the lit relief (207, 1.3:1); at 390x844 ten lines on it (178 to 207, down to 1.15:1), the sign-off's own telephone number among them. The critique's third means, then its second. *Position*: on a wide frame the camera turns off the table as it rises, down and to the left, and the table ends beside the sign-off, in the same band of the frame's height (its rim 54 to 83% across and 22 to 51% down on the design's frame), with the court's dark floor under both for the quiet lines; on a phone held upright there is no beside, so the phone's lens does it — the front shifts the other way and the field closes — and the table rises to the upper part of the frame at the screen's width, with the sign-off coming to stand whole beneath it; a tablet held upright is laid out as a phone is but has the room, so there the same lens puts the table on the right, beside the sign-off (under the phone's frame its colophon, which is shorter than the frame, ended with the headline level with the table's foot and the lens closed over the page's last sixteenth of a viewport). The move is over in three-quarters of a viewport (it was nine-tenths), so the frame is composed before the words arrive in it. *The lens*: a colophon is taller than most frames, and on a phone, a phone on its side or a very short laptop its lines still ride up through the place the table stands. Nothing in a frame can be moved out of the way of a block of text that crosses all of it, so there the light goes, as it does in a cinema when the credits come: the lens closes down over the whole frame, 3.2 stops in scene-linear light before the print's curve (the land sinks into the toe and stays the last warm thing in a dark house — never a ground laid behind the words), as the first line that will cross the table comes within a twenty-fifth of the frame of it, and stays closed while any such line has still to pass. The page says where the WORDS of each visible line are (a range over its glyphs, not its box: a link's row is 44 px for the thumb), the table says where its rim stands on the screen through the camera it is drawn with, and the lens compares the two every frame. Lines beside the table ask for nothing, so on a desk, on every laptop measured and on a tablet held upright the film ends with the land lit; on a phone, and a phone on its side, with the land an ember under the colophon. The privacy control in the colophon is at its row's strength (it was 60%: 3.98:1 on the stone at its lightest).
  - THE CLERESTORY'S SHAFTS STOP CLEAR OF THE RUG (WindowLight.tsx). Found on the new last frame: a thin black rod at the foot of the picture, on the rug, in the pool of light under the fourth window. Not an object: the shafts are prisms drawn by their back faces against the room's depth, and each ended exactly where the sill's edge meets the marble. The rug lies 4 cm proud of the marble, so for its last 4 cm the prism was under the rug's surface, its far faces lost the depth test, and a bar of rug 4 cm wide and the window's width long stood unlit in the middle of the light (found by drawing every object alone: only the floor, the rug and the shaft cover those pixels; and by turning off each of the rug's maps in turn, which changed nothing). The prisms now stop 6 cm above the floor.
- Verified: tsc clean, eslint 8 warnings (all older than this pass), vitest 310 in 24 files. By capture on the production build (build 41): the film's tail at 16 sizes of frame (304 frames, 4,841 text nodes) with nothing under AA at rest and no colophon line on the lit table (66 lines under AA only while the map chapter's list is mid-dissolve); type on 27 routes at 6 sizes, one family and on the scale; menus, the door and the console clean; the film swept at 12 wide sizes (252 frames) and the narrow ones (149 frames) with no line on a ground over its limit; every line at its own colour and opacity at 7 sizes; 23 inner routes at 3 sizes (14,090 text boxes) at AA.

The experience E2E suite and the frame times were not re-run in this pass (the suite ran with the next entry: 36/36, its four new tests of the film's last frame among them). ## [2026-10-01..03 IST] Issue: the client's ten notes on the film (a fully flat roof, a warm dusk, a lit hall, the founder's portrait and its nameplate)
- Found: ten notes with screenshots taken at 1920x1078, each measured on the build they were taken from (frames in scratchpad c7/before_*):
  1. "the blue shadows of this look cheap": the dusk haze was the film's navy (`HAZE_NIGHT` #131E33), written for a dusk whose sky was a navy clear colour. Under the photographed sunset, with the print opened up for the evening, the far land printed (6, 19, 46) — a saturated ink-blue band under an amber sky — and the print's shadows leaned teal.
  2. "this looks darker make it less dark" (the forecourt): the sky's fill fell to 0.22 of itself with the evening, so the shaded flank printed at a luma of 48 (64 by day) and the shaded lawn, the hedges and the car at 9.
  3. "remove the top part of the roof and replace it with a flat roof" — and, of a first attempt that took off the spire and left the cupola as a flat-topped belvedere on the hipped roof: "not this i said i want fully flat roof".
  4. "the founder's hair is covered with the lighting": the picture lamp's tube shipped at an emissive strength of 5.5 (four times the hall's bloom threshold) and the beam's sheet of light was still at six-tenths strength over the hair.
  5. "the darkness is much more than required and it gives out creepy vibes": the turn to the first table (leg 0.13) was made at a house-light level of 0.05 with no copy on screen (frame mean luma 14).
  6. "the darkness behind the kartikeya water front text is a bit too much": the lens's edge at the first table was 2.4 stops.
  7. "this also looks too dark" (the second table): its room stood at 0.15 for the sake of its copy's column.
  8. "remove this reflection on founder's image, reduce the intensity of the light": the glass drew the entry's three arched windows and the lamp's bar by reflected ray, and took the room's probe at a gain of 2.4.
  9. "the nameplate is not visible clearly ... it must create respect": brass with dark engraving; under the picture light the brass printed as a pale bar and the title line stood about 5 px high.
  10. "remove the hover effect on this image": a warm additive plane over the canvas (opacity 0.1 on arrival, 0.6 under the pointer).
- Fix applied:
  - A FULLY FLAT ROOF (tools/blender/build_estate_v7.py `build_roof`, bake_estate_ao_v7.py, tools/gltf/estate_bounds_v7.py; exterior_estate_v7.glb re-baked and shipped, 12.0 MB). The hipped slate roof, its cross gables, the cupola and the spire are gone: one slate flat from wall head to wall head behind the balustraded parapet, a stone kerb round it and its middle raised one shallow step. The pediments are the thickness of the wall they stand on (they ran 3 m back, two pitched stone roofs in miniature; `PEDIMENT_DEPTH` 0.8). The flat is riven, not polished (`POLISH.MAT_Roof_Slate` rough 1.7, env 0.4): polished as the pitched roof was it mirrored the 14-degree sun as one white glare from behind the house. The house now ends at its urns, 12.46 m (`ESTATE_ROOF_TOP`; the `cupola` box is gone from estateBounds.ts and the lens's house hull).
  - THE CAMERA, FOR A LOWER HOUSE (cameraPath.ts). On a wide frame the crane and the holdings beats aim 4 m lower (`ROOFLINE_AIM` 12), so the house holds the middle of the frame its spire used to reach into. The approach's aim curve is still drawn from the old 16 m (`APPROACH_AIM_FROM`) and the difference is blended out over its first segment, so every frame from the flank beat to the door is the frame it was (lowering the shared beat had moved the picture 12 px at leg 0.8, onto a lit window). An upright screen keeps the old aim altogether (`FILM_TARGET_CURVE_UPRIGHT`): there the copy stands above the house, and lifting the house put the holdings' gloss across its lit windows.
  - THE DUSK (WorldCanvas, FilmGrade). The haze is the sunset's own lower sky (`HAZE_NIGHT` #37302E: the far land now prints about (45, 34, 28), under a sky's foot of (57, 46, 37)); the sky's fill RISES with the evening instead of dying (`EVENING_FILL` 1.3, from the day's blue to a warm `FILL_EVENING`), the night takes less of it (`NIGHT_FILL_LOSS` 0.3), and the print's shadows come off their teal lean and are lifted off black at dusk (`EVENING_SHADOW_TINT`). Forecourt frame: mean luma 44 -> 58, the shaded flank 48 -> about 75.
  - THE APPROACH'S COPY WAITS FOR THE DOOR (ChapterFade `data-fade-after`, lensFilter `APPROACH_ND.from`). Found while re-checking the lighter dusk every fiftieth of the leg: the pane was held from leg 0.76, and the flank's lit windows passed straight behind it ("The door is open." across a row of them at 0.78, 1.5:1). The copy now develops in place a viewport later, as the camera comes onto the front (whole by leg 0.86), and the foot grad comes on just before it.
  - THE HALL'S LIGHT (hallLight.ts, journey.ts `establishCopyGone`, InteriorStage). The house lights stay down only while the establishing copy stands on the plaster, then come up through the turn (`HALL_WALK` 0.62 at the turn beat, full at the table). The second table's room is up from 0.15 to 0.36 — as far as its hologram allows: it alone stands against ivory, and at 0.5 the plan's own title is a ghost (seen at four levels).
  - THE LENS'S EDGE AT THE TABLES (lensFilter `STATION_ND`): 2.4 / 0 / 2.3 -> 2.1 / 1.2 / 1.8, each what its own copy needs; the numeral over each block is ivory, not gilt (small gilt wanted a ground under 80, which is what part of the old density was buying).

## [2026-10-03..04 IST] Issue: the refinement brief ("make the existing experience more believable, not more impressive"; everything in it except the stations, which stay as they are)
- Found: the client's consolidated brief (two consultant audits) against build 48. P0: the estate's props and light gave it away as a render — a car that was a block, palms that were white poles, a porch lit by three invisible panels the size of the door and its windows (a lit sticker round each opening), every room lit alike behind the glass, a blur that softened the house it was meant to isolate, a kerb laid across the drive, a car parked into the steps. P1: the passage through the door was a cut through black ("the current black-void transition should be replaced with a continuous physical/cinematic entry through the architecture"); chapter labels, numerals and hairlines over the film's copy; a hall at noon (white-hot panes, sunbeams) behind an estate at dusk, with stepped seams up its columns and blobs for bases. Scope asked of the user before starting, a pasted brief not being an order: all of it, in the brief's order; the holograms untouched; the door continuous; generated models allowed.
- Why it matters: a $130K film is judged on whether the place is believed, and each of these is what an eye that knows buildings, lenses or cars sees first.
- Fix applied — EXTERIOR: a thin lens in place of the distance blur (`LensFocus.tsx`: focused on the face of the house the axis meets, f/2 on a 24 mm gate; the house sharp, only the far land soft); one bronze lantern hung from the portico's soffit with a shadow-casting point light in place of the three rect lights (`PorticoLantern.tsx`); rooms lit unevenly at believable levels, with drapes, and exempt from contact occlusion (`exteriorWindows.ts`, `ESTATE_EMITTER`); ashlar joints in world space; the drive in scanned gravel and the terrace in scanned flags (Poly Haven CC0, `tools/gltf/ph_surfaces_v7.py`); a neutral fill after sundown, bloom threshold 1.0, motes quietened. THE CLIENT'S OWN GENERATED MODELS (Tripo, from prompts written for the film, saved to `<repo>/Tripo`): `tools/blender/tripo_assets_v7.py` sizes, grounds and lightens each into a library, and `build_estate_v7.py` places a saloon at the steps and another across the carriage ring, a coupe opposite, coconut palms in the eight groves and royal palms on the forecourt, and the lantern's body (its wall bracket cut away, a mask of its glass made its emission), each with a scripted fallback. Cars stand along the ring's outer kerb, nose the way a ring is driven, not across the carriageway; the car at the steps is on the gravel clear of the stair's pedestal (its tail was in the podium wall, a wheel on the lawn); the ring's outer kerb stops where the apron and the avenue run through it; clipped yews.
- Fix applied — WHAT FACES THE SKY IS WEATHERED (`exteriorSurfaces.ts`, `SKY_FACE`; found in the end-to-end review). On the revolve (leg 0.26 to 0.34, the sun 14 degrees up behind the house) the top of every roof-balustrade rail and pedestal cap printed as a white strip — 2,491 pixels of the 1920 frame above a luma of 225 at leg 0.30 — strip lighting along a parapet. Measured on the running build before anything was changed: gone with the trim's roughness at full (289), none with the sun off; the honed finish (`POLISH`, roughness x0.62) mirroring the low sun at a grazing angle. A face that looks at the sky now has weathered stone's roughness (0.94), by its own slope, taken from the world position's derivatives (which point at the eye, so a soffit seen from below is left alone); every upright face keeps its hone. 300 pixels after, all of them the horizon; the hero 52 -> 43.
- Fix applied — THE DOOR: `doorway.ts` gains a second style, `through`: the leaves open on the hall itself, drawn from the camera's own eye into a buffer and shown by a panel behind the leaves (`doorPortal.ts`, `HallPortal.tsx`, `DoorwayRig.tsx`); the camera makes one eased move over the fountain, through the opening and on to the threshold frame; the sets change behind the doorway with the room already filling the frame, the print, bloom, vignette, header band and contact occlusion having gone over to the room's by then. The dark threshold stays for a low-tier device, a hall still loading, `?door=threshold`, and the way out. What the first live runs MEASURED, and what was done: (1) 33 programs compiled in one frame of 11.9 s at the swap — the page's React tree named the hall a frame before the canvas's own root had made the scene the hall, and that frame drew the room under the sun, the lantern and the fog; the flag is now written by a layout effect inside the canvas (`SceneLegMark`). (2) Four shadow programs compiled as the door opened — a pass drawn before the frame left the room's two lights in the scene's shared light state; the pass is now drawn from inside the frame's render (the panel's `onBeforeRender`). (3) The panels' and the map pins' programs compiled on first use — warmed ahead (`warmProgramsUnder`; an instanced mesh with instance colours is its own kind). (4) One-frame faults at the swap, each invisible under the old dark: the header's band zeroed by the estate's lighting as it unmounted, the picture light aimed a frame late (on the carpet), the establishing copy's filter on at full at the cut, occlusion, bloom and vignette stepping — each moved into the commit or onto the passage's ramp. The pass's frustum is cropped to the doorway's place on the screen, so from the forecourt the room costs a few per cent of a frame. The door's caption leaves with the first steps.
- Fix applied — THE HALL: `tools/blender/refine_hall_v7.py` (Attic column bases, the stair's apron frames); `bake_lightmap.py` unrolls every pilaster shaft to one lightmap island (their stepped seams were an unwrap fault, not geometry). The light re-baked as TWO families at 384 spp — the lamps; the windows and oculus — and mixed at 1.6 : 0.55 (`tools/gltf/mix_hall_passes.py`; another balance is a re-mix, not a re-bake). The panes are the sky after sunset (`HallModel.DUSK_GLASS`) and the shafts a fifth of what they were and cool (`WindowLight.SHAFT`). With the shafts no longer the room's fill, each of the ivory room's beats has its own house-light level (`hallLight.HALL_DOWN`: 0.11 at the threshold, the establishing shot and on the climb, 0.08 at the portrait, 0.09 at the court, 0.07 at the map table; all were 0.05), and the second table's room is 0.34 (0.36: the sconce beside it leads more of its wall's light now). A fully lamp-led mix (4.5 : 0.1) was built and measured first, and dropped: the wall behind the tables' copy came out 2.7 to 3.7 times brighter and the second table's plan washed out. THE READING PAGES ON THE HALL were measured against their build-41 frames: /hall, a wide frame of the whole room, had two-thirds of its light (frame mean 22.0 against 32.3 at 1536x730) and takes 0.07 of its own (`HALL_READING_ROOM`: 30.0; at 0.08 its caption's small lines over the marble were down to 4.4-4.6:1 on a phone and a tablet); the rest look at a wall or stand a surface over the room, measured 0.81 to 0.98 of what they were, and keep 0.05.
- Fix applied — TYPE: every chapter label is for screen readers only; station numerals and hairlines removed; hero and holdings re-set on the frame unit; the revolution's block moved 10% down the frame after the deep-focus lens changed what stands behind it. Then, by the strict battery on the screens the film is actually seen on, after the hall's light had moved: the establishing statement's "side;" at 4.21:1 on a laptop (1536x730) and 4.23 at 1280x593, the coda's gloss 4.46 and 4.36, the second table's place line 4.43 and 4.23 (1280x593, 844x390), a place in the map's list 4.45 — the levels above, and those three lines in full ivory (they were 88 to 90% of it). The map's gloss keeps both of its claims ("a model ..., not a survey: a pin marks a district, not a place on the ground"): an earlier trim in this pass had cut the second, and the suite's own test of that sentence caught it.
- Verified (build 71; RTX 4070 and the AMD integrated GPU): tsc clean, lint at its 8 older warnings, vitest 365 in 26 files, Playwright experience **37/37** (6.0 min; the front-door test runs both styles). Door, by CDP screencast (a screenshot holds the page a second a frame): 277 frames in 4.6 s (60.3 fps, worst gap 30 ms), 0 programs compiled across the passage (113 before, 109 after), dark coverage 0, exposure 1 throughout, mean luminance either side of the swap 0.1683 / 0.1678 / 0.1679 (the first run: 0.1246 / 0.1569 / 0.1213, and one frame of 11.9 s). Text, each node at its own colour and opacity against the brightest tenth of its ground: the film's 17 resting frames at eight sizes of screen (1440x900, 1536x730, 1920x945, 1280x593, 844x390, 820x1180, 390x844, 390x664): 1,621 text boxes, none under AA. Exterior, every 0.02 of the leg at 1920x1080 (build 67; 50 frames, 206 lines of copy and header): weakest 5.3:1. Inner pages: 23 routes top to bottom at 1536x730 and at 390x844, 5,221 text boxes, none under AA; /hall also at 1920x1080, 844x390 and 820x1180. Hall against build 48 at 1920x1080, each whole frame in linear light: the tables 1.00, 0.86 and 0.87, the walk 1.04, the portrait 1.07, the court 0.90, the map table 0.81; the establishing shot 0.43 and the climb 0.56, which is the sun's panes and shafts gone from those two frames. Frame rate, in full Chromium's headless mode (a presenting browser's back-pressure, paced by this panel's 165 Hz; see the correction below): the RTX 4070 at 165 fps at every beat at 1920x1080 (worst frame 7 ms) and 133 to 152 outside, 165 inside, at 2880x1800; the integrated Radeon at 66 to 71 outside and 91 to 111 inside at 1920x1080, and at 35 to 41 outside and 52 to 62 inside at 2880x1620. Before/after sheets: `C:/dev/Blender/_brief_2026-10-03/`. Estate 1,773,278 triangles drawn (1,449,992: +22%, the cars and palms) and 16.5 MB (12.2 at HEAD); hall 913,513 triangles (864,037) and 26.5 MB (25.6).
- A CORRECTION TO THIS LOG'S FRAME-RATE FIGURES, AND WHAT THE RIGHT MEASURE FOUND. Every "60 fps (p95 16.8 ms) on the AMD iGPU" above was a median and a 95th percentile taken in Playwright's headless SHELL, and neither can see one long frame in a hundred. By its worst frame, a still exterior there shows a frame of 0.85 s every 1.75 s (646 frames in 20 s). That is the shell, not the film — it lets the page run on at 60 Hz while it batches the context's GPU work, then stalls to drain it — and full Chromium's own headless mode (`channel: 'chromium'`) shows no frame over 50 ms in 20 s. Measured there, un-throttled (scratch c9/pace_probe.mjs, c9/cost_probe.mjs), the figures are the ones above. So on an integrated GPU the film holds 60 at a 1920x1080 buffer, with about a tenth to spare outside, and does NOT at a HiDPI one: 35 to 41 fps on the exterior at 2880x1620, the pixel count of a 13-inch MacBook's screen. It is the pixels (14.9 ms a frame at 2.1 MP, 25.5 at 4.7) and not this pass's additions: with the generated models hidden the frame is 1.2 ms shorter, without the lantern's shadow lookup 0.9 (2.2 at the door), without the sun's shadow pass 3.4. And it is older than this pass: the canvas takes a pixel ratio of up to 2 on the 'high' tier, and the tier is decided from the first ninety frames, before the estate is resident (`useDeviceTier`), so nearly every device is 'high'. NOT FIXED HERE: the fix is a resolution governor (step the pixel ratio down while the measured frame is over budget; one way; with a check that the step helped, so a display capped at 30 Hz is left alone), which changes what every visitor's device draws and cannot be verified on the hardware it is for from this machine. Flagged.
- Flagged for review: **yes** — (1) the way OUT of the hall still goes by the dark threshold (a return; the estate would have to be drawn twice): say if it should be continuous too. (2) The hall's dusk is a reading of "reduce beam/glowing windows" plus continuity through the open door; it is the largest change of look here, and the raw light passes are kept (`C:/dev/Blender/_v7ref_lamps`, `_v7ref_sky`) so the balance can be moved in minutes. (3) Generated palms are solid leaf, so only the palms the film passes near are the client's models (8,000 and 5,000 triangles each); the avenue and the belt keep the scripted palm. (4) `Tripo/` (the five source GLBs, 35 MB) is untracked: commit it or ignore it, but the ship script reads it. (5) Nothing is committed. (6) Plot selection on the holograms still awaits a decision. (7) HiDPI screens on integrated GPUs run the exterior at 35 to 41 fps (the correction above): recommend the resolution governor, verified on a MacBook before it ships.

## [2026-10-04 IST] Issue: the rest of the refinement brief — the stations as site models, the way out by the same door, softened stone, the avenue's palms, the approach's camera (the user: "do everything in the doc even the hologram one i give u permission")
- Found: asked how much of the brief's own document had been done, the count was 24 of 36 instructions. Not done: section E (replace the "sci-fi" projection with "a more tactile architectural-model treatment", keep rotation, examination and the click through, "reduce visual layers around the model"), and the camera distances (C3: "avoid camera distances that expose the limitations of the model without providing photographic intimacy"). Partly done: the building's over-clean edges (A2), painted wood against plaster (A3), the far palms (A5: the avenue still the scripted pole), the way OUT of the hall (C6: a cut through black), and the tables on a phone, where the plan ran off the right edge of every upright screen (D5).
- Why it matters: the brief's test is whether the place is believed. A plan of light floating over a period table is the one object in the hall that says "screen"; a second cut through black undoes the continuous door the visitor has just come through; and a camera that looks down on a bare flat roof from 20 m is the model's weakest face at its most legible distance.
- Fix applied — THE STATIONS (`stationStyle.ts`, `StationModel.tsx`, `ProjectStation.tsx`): each plan is a site model — its plots raised ivory blocks (20 to 26 mm, the open land 4, cut from the plan's own cells: `tools/blender/make_plan_model_geo.py`, about 3,500 triangles and 0.2 MB a plan) on a board of two book-matched walnut leaves with a brass bezel and an ebonised plaque carrying the project's name in gilt capitals, the face drawn from the plan's own artwork (`tools/gltf/make_plan_models.py`: ivory plots with a trace of their colour, sepia ink, sage land; KTX2, 0.8 to 1.4 MB), on a turned brass stand, lit by the chandelier's key through the hall's own probe (`MODEL_LIGHT`, `MODEL_BRASS`). It stands where the projection stood, turns with the table (`TURNTABLE_Sn`: the tables carry authored rest angles, so the model spins by the table's angle less the first angle seen — it sat rotated on the second and third until that was found) and a click still opens the project. The projector, beam, pane, labels and idle drift are not drawn. Tried and dropped on the way: a black lacquer ground in a gilt frame (read as a screen) and bright brass (read as gold plate; now `#7b6540`, rough, at half the key). THE PROJECTION IS KEPT WHOLE: `?stations=hologram` draws `StationDressing` exactly as before (its code and assets are untouched; measured against build 71, the same frame), because physical models were rejected once before (2026-09-30). `DEFAULT_STATION_STYLE` is the one line that decides.
- Fix applied — THE TABLES ON A PHONE AND A TABLET (`phoneFraming.ts`): the table beats take a lens of their own on an upright screen — twice the field and the front shifted two-fifths of the width on a phone (`PHONE_FRAMING`), half as much again as the plain lens and three-tenths on a tablet (`TABLET_TABLES`, mixed by width) — so the model, the table and its pedestal stand whole in the frame under the copy. Under the projection the frames are left as they were.
- Fix applied — THE WAY OUT (`doorway.ts` `throughOutChannels`, `THROUGH_OUT_MS` 3800; `WorldCanvas.tsx` Rig; `doorPortal.ts` `portalWanted`; `globals.css`): the way in, backwards — the camera draws back from the hall's first frame through the open leaves, the sets change behind the doorway a tenth of the way in with the room still filling the frame, the leaves close and the camera settles on the door's own frame; the caption waits for the page to be let go. By the dark threshold only when the estate has never been drawn, on a low tier, or with `?door=threshold`. SIX ONE-FRAME FAULTS, each found on a screencast or a profile and none visible in a count of programs alone: (1) five shadow depth and distance programs compiled on the frame the sets change (130 ms): three draws a frame's shadow maps before it sets the frame's lights up, so they were keyed on the hall's lights — that one frame is drawn without its shadow pass (`SceneLegMark`); (2) the dust's material disposed on the way in and built again (`Motes` stays mounted, `shown`); (3) the nameplate and the tables' hit boxes drawn once under the estate's lights as the passage ended, three programs and a frame of 0.6 s (`HallPortal.close` hides every root of the hall); (4) the room without its window shafts, the picture light's beam and the nameplate for one frame at the change — the stage hides itself when React hides the hall, a callback before the doorway's pass shows it again; the door under the stair fell from 26.5 to 9.6 and came back (`HallPortal` shows every root, every frame); (5) a frame of 67 ms just after: the sky panorama loaded and uploaded a second time for the background's cube (46.8 ms of texSubImage2D in a CPU profile of that frame), its prefiltered copy made again (6.5) and the prefilter's programs built again (4.7) — both skies are loaded once and kept while the visitor is inside (`SkyBackground`, `armed`, `made`); (6) the establishing copy's half stop of lens filter gone in one frame, the lower left of the picture up to 55% brighter from one frame to the next — the estate's loop leaves the filter to the hall's stage, which eases it away, until the passage is over (`hallsFilter`).
- Fix applied — THE ESTATE (`tools/blender/build_estate_v7.py`, `tripo_assets_v7.py`, `ExteriorModel.tsx` `POLISH`): the hard arrises of dressed stone eased (`ease_arrises`: a single bevel of 8 to 16 mm on the convex edges of the portico's trim, steps, podium walls, quoins, window trim, string courses, pediments, parapet, balustrades, fountain, kerbs, coping and planters — the edge a mason leaves, and the line of light a low sun finds on it); a nosing on every tread of the portico's and the garden's steps (28 mm proud, 45 thick); the avenue's 36 palms are the client's royal palm at a 2,600-triangle level in place of the scripted pole; window joinery and dark wood drier and less mirror-like, the awnings' canvas toned. About 110,000 triangles more; the GLB is 16.9 MB.
- Fix applied — THE APPROACH'S CAMERA (`cameraPath.ts`, the `forecourt` beat): it rounded the front-left corner 20 m out and two and a half metres over the parapet, looking down, and a third of the frame was the flat roof. Now 10.6 m up (under the parapet's 12.5), 27 m out, on 41 degrees in place of 45, aimed a metre higher: the lit front as a three-quarter elevation against the evening sky; the next beat comes down to 10.0 m so the descent is one way to the door. The old values are in the beat's comment. The revolution's pass along the flank was reviewed and left as the storyboard has it.
- Fix applied — THE SUN'S SHADOW MAP IS DRAWN WHEN SOMETHING IN IT HAS MOVED (`softSunShadows.ts` `SUN_SHADOW_CADENCE`, `sunShadowDue`; `WorldCanvas.tsx` `ExteriorLighting`). Found measuring the heavier estate: on the integrated GPU the exterior read 50 to 54 frames a second at 1920x1080 where build 71 read 66 to 71. Those figures are taken at this panel's 165 Hz, in steps of 6.06 ms, and a frame that crosses 12.1 ms reads as 18.2; with the frame-rate limit off (`--disable-frame-rate-limit --disable-gpu-vsync`, `c10/tri_probe.mjs`) the true throughput was 59.0, 60.2 and 63.5 at the cover, over the garden and at the door — the edge of a 60 Hz panel — and the new stone and palms were 0.4 ms of it. What cost a quarter of the frame was the sun's shadow pass: 1.85 million triangles (the tree belt alone 1.1 million) drawn into a 4096 map on every frame, for a sun that does not move, a shadow camera that is a fixed box and casters of which only the door's two leaves ever do (the trees sway in the picture, not in the depth pass). The map is now drawn for a second and a half after the light mounts or the estate arrives, on every frame of a passage through the door and half a second after, whenever it has none, and twice a second as a net; nothing else.
